\title{Catalan numbers and binary trees}
\author{}
\date{}
\begin{document}
\maketitle
\section*{Definitions}
\paragraph{Catalan.} \par\noindent\refstepcounter{definingequation}\label{defeq:1}\paperlineanchor{defeq:1}\textup{(\paperlink{defeq:1}{D1})} \(\operatorname{catalan}\left(0\right) = 1\).
\par\noindent\refstepcounter{definingequation}\label{defeq:2}\paperlineanchor{defeq:2}\textup{(\paperlink{defeq:2}{D2})} Let \(n\) be a natural number. Then \(\operatorname{catalan}(n + 1) = \sum_{i\in \operatorname{Fin}(n + 1)}(\operatorname{catalan}(\operatorname{val}(i)) \cdot \operatorname{catalan}(n - \operatorname{val}(i)))\).

\paragraph{Pairwise node.} \par\noindent\refstepcounter{definingequation}\label{defeq:3}\paperlineanchor{defeq:3}\textup{(\paperlink{defeq:3}{D3})} Let \(a\) be a finite subset of \(\operatorname{Tree}\left(\operatorname{Unit}\right)\). Let \(b\) be a finite subset of \(\operatorname{Tree}\left(\operatorname{Unit}\right)\). Then \(\operatorname{pairwise\,Node}(a,\allowbreak{} b) = \operatorname{map}(\operatorname{Function\mathord{:}Embedding\mathord{:}mk}(x \mapsto \operatorname{node}(\operatorname{unit},\allowbreak{} \pi_1(x),\allowbreak{} \pi_2(x))),\allowbreak{} \operatorname{sprod}(a,\allowbreak{} b))\).

\paragraph{Trees of num nodes equals.} \par\noindent\refstepcounter{definingequation}\label{defeq:4}\paperlineanchor{defeq:4}\textup{(\paperlink{defeq:4}{D4})} \(\operatorname{trees\,Of\,Num\,Nodes\,Eq}\left(0\right) = \{\operatorname{Tree\mathord{:}nil}\}\).
\par\noindent\refstepcounter{definingequation}\label{defeq:5}\paperlineanchor{defeq:5}\textup{(\paperlink{defeq:5}{D5})} Let \(n\) be a natural number. Then \(\operatorname{trees\,Of\,Num\,Nodes\,Eq}(n + 1) = \bigcup_{\mathit{ijh}\in \operatorname{attach}(\operatorname{antidiagonal}(n))} \operatorname{pairwise\,Node}(\operatorname{trees\,Of\,Num\,Nodes\,Eq}(\pi_1(\mathit{ijh})),\allowbreak{} \operatorname{trees\,Of\,Num\,Nodes\,Eq}(\pi_2(\mathit{ijh})))\).

\paragraph{Gosper catalan.} \par\noindent\refstepcounter{definingequation}\label{defeq:6}\paperlineanchor{defeq:6}\textup{(\paperlink{defeq:6}{D6})} Let \(n\) and \(j\) be natural numbers. In \(\mathbb{Q}\), \(\operatorname{gosper\,Catalan}(n,\allowbreak{} j) = \frac{\binom{2 \cdot j}{j} \cdot \binom{2 \cdot \max\{n - j,0\}}{\max\{n - j,0\}} \cdot (2 \cdot j - n)}{2 \cdot n \cdot (n + 1)}\). The quotient is defined to be zero when its denominator is zero.

\subsection*{Prerequisites}
\begin{description}
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:41646447726f75702e6578697374734164644f664c45}\paperlink{premise:41646447726f75702e6578697374734164644f664c45}{P1.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:41646447726f75702e6578697374734164644f664c45}\textbf{Exists sum of at most.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Add\,Group}\left(\alpha\right)\). Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}_{1}\) of type \(\operatorname{LE}\left(\alpha\right)\). Then \(\operatorname{Exists\,Add\,Of\,LE}\left(\alpha\right)\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:46696e2e69734c74}\paperlink{premise:46696e2e69734c74}{P2.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:46696e2e69734c74}\textbf{Is less than.} Let \(n\) be a natural number. Let \(\mathit{self}\) be an element of \(\operatorname{Fin}(n)\). Then \(\operatorname{val}(\mathit{self}) < n\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:46696e2e69735f6c65}\paperlink{premise:46696e2e69735f6c65}{P3.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:46696e2e69735f6c65}\textbf{Is at most.} Let \(n\) be a natural number. Let \(i\) be an element of \(\operatorname{Fin}(n + 1)\). Then \(\operatorname{val}(i) \le n\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:46696e2e76616c5f65715f7a65726f}\paperlink{premise:46696e2e76616c5f65715f7a65726f}{P4.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:46696e2e76616c5f65715f7a65726f}\textbf{Val equals zero.} Let \(a\) be an element of \(\operatorname{Fin}(1)\). Then \(\operatorname{val}(a) = 0\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:46696e7365742e486173416e7469646961676f6e616c2e6d656d5f616e7469646961676f6e616c}\paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e486173416e7469646961676f6e616c2e6d656d5f616e7469646961676f6e616c}{P5.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:46696e7365742e486173416e7469646961676f6e616c2e6d656d5f616e7469646961676f6e616c}\textbf{Membership antidiagonal.} Let \(A\) be a type. Let \(\mathit{inst}\) be an element of \(\operatorname{Add\,Monoid}\left(A\right)\). Fix a structure \(\mathit{self}\) of type \(\operatorname{Has\,Antidiagonal}\left(A\right)\). Let \(n\) be an element of \(A\). Let \(a\) be an element of \(A \times A\). Then \(a \in \operatorname{antidiagonal}\left(n\right) \iff \pi_1(a) + \pi_2(a) = n\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:46696e7365742e4e61742e73756d5f616e7469646961676f6e616c5f65715f73756d5f72616e67655f73756363}\paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e4e61742e73756d5f616e7469646961676f6e616c5f65715f73756d5f72616e67655f73756363}{P6.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:46696e7365742e4e61742e73756d5f616e7469646961676f6e616c5f65715f73756d5f72616e67655f73756363}\textbf{Sum antidiagonal equals sum range successor.} Let \(M\) be a type. Equip \(M\) with a commutative additive monoid structure. Let \(f\) be a function of type \(\mathbb{N} \to \mathbb{N} \to M\). Let \(n\) be a natural number. Then \(\sum_{\mathit{ij}\in \operatorname{antidiagonal}(n)}(f(\pi_1(\mathit{ij}),\allowbreak{} \pi_2(\mathit{ij}))) = \sum_{0\le k<n + 1}(f(k,\allowbreak{} n - k))\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:46696e7365742e616e7469646961676f6e616c2e6673745f6c65}\paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e616e7469646961676f6e616c2e6673745f6c65}{P7.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:46696e7365742e616e7469646961676f6e616c2e6673745f6c65}\textbf{Fst at most.} Let \(A\) be a type. Equip \(A\) with a commutative additive monoid structure and a partial order. Let \(\mathit{inst}_{2}\) be a proof of \(\operatorname{Canonically\,Ordered\,Add}\left(A\right)\). Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}_{3}\) of type \(\operatorname{Has\,Antidiagonal}\left(A\right)\). Let \(n\) be an element of \(A\). Let \(\mathit{kl}\) be an element of \(A \times A\). Assume \(\mathit{kl} \in \operatorname{antidiagonal}\left(n\right)\). Then \(\pi_1(\mathit{kl}) \le n\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:46696e7365742e616e7469646961676f6e616c2e736e645f6c65}\paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e616e7469646961676f6e616c2e736e645f6c65}{P8.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:46696e7365742e616e7469646961676f6e616c2e736e645f6c65}\textbf{Snd at most.} Let \(A\) be a type. Equip \(A\) with a commutative additive monoid structure and a partial order. Let \(\mathit{inst}_{2}\) be a proof of \(\operatorname{Canonically\,Ordered\,Add}\left(A\right)\). Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}_{3}\) of type \(\operatorname{Has\,Antidiagonal}\left(A\right)\). Let \(n\) be an element of \(A\). Let \(\mathit{kl}\) be an element of \(A \times A\). Assume \(\mathit{kl} \in \operatorname{antidiagonal}\left(n\right)\). Then \(\pi_2(\mathit{kl}) \le n\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:46696e7365742e636172645f6269556e696f6e}\paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e636172645f6269556e696f6e}{P9.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:46696e7365742e636172645f6269556e696f6e}\textbf{Cardinality bi union.} Let \(\iota\) and \(M\) be types. Let \(s\) be a finite subset of \(\iota\). Assume equality on \(M\) is decidable. Let \(t\) be a family of finite subsets of \(M\) indexed by \(\iota\). Assume \(\operatorname{Pairwise\,Disjoint}\left(\operatorname{underlying\,set}(s), t\right)\). Then \(\left|\bigcup_{i\in s} t(i)\right| = \sum_{u\in s}\left(\left|t\left(u\right)\right|\right)\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:46696e7365742e636172645f6d6170}\paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e636172645f6d6170}{P10.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:46696e7365742e636172645f6d6170}\textbf{Cardinality map.} Let \(\alpha\) and \(\beta\) be types. Let \(s\) be a finite subset of \(\alpha\). Let \(f\) be an element of \(\operatorname{Embedding}\left(\alpha, \beta\right)\). Then \(\left|\operatorname{map}\left(f, s\right)\right| = \left|s\right|\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:46696e7365742e636172645f70726f64756374}\paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e636172645f70726f64756374}{P11.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:46696e7365742e636172645f70726f64756374}\textbf{Cardinality product.} Let \(\alpha\) and \(\beta\) be types. Let \(s\) be a finite subset of \(\alpha\). Let \(t\) be a finite subset of \(\beta\). Then \(\left|\operatorname{sprod}\left(s, t\right)\right| = \left|s\right| \cdot \left|t\right|\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:46696e7365742e636172645f73696e676c65746f6e}\paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e636172645f73696e676c65746f6e}{P12.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:46696e7365742e636172645f73696e676c65746f6e}\textbf{Cardinality singleton.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Let \(a\) be an element of \(\alpha\). Then \(\left|\{a\}\right| = 1\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:46696e7365742e6469736a6f696e745f6c656674}\paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e6469736a6f696e745f6c656674}{P13.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:46696e7365742e6469736a6f696e745f6c656674}\textbf{Disjoint left.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Let \(s\) and \(t\) be finite subsets of \(\alpha\). Then \(\operatorname{Disjoint}(s,\allowbreak{} t) \iff (\forall a\, :\, \alpha,\; a \in s \implies a \notin t)\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:46696e7365742e657874}\paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e657874}{P14.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:46696e7365742e657874}\textbf{Ext.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Let \(s_{1}\) and \(s_{2}\) be finite subsets of \(\alpha\). Assume \(\forall a\, :\, \alpha,\; a \in s_{1} \iff a \in s_{2}\). Then \(s_{1} = s_{2}\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:46696e7365742e6d656d5f617474616368}\paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e6d656d5f617474616368}{P15.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:46696e7365742e6d656d5f617474616368}\textbf{Membership attach.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Let \(s\) be a finite subset of \(\alpha\). Let \(x\) be an element of \(s\). Then \(x \in \operatorname{attach}\left(s\right)\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:46696e7365742e6d656d5f6269556e696f6e}\paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e6d656d5f6269556e696f6e}{P16.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:46696e7365742e6d656d5f6269556e696f6e}\textbf{Membership bi union.} Let \(\alpha\) and \(\beta\) be types. Let \(s\) be a finite subset of \(\alpha\). Let \(t\) be a family of finite subsets of \(\beta\) indexed by \(\alpha\). Assume equality on \(\beta\) is decidable. Let \(b\) be an element of \(\beta\). Then \(b \in \bigcup_{i\in s} t(i) \iff \left(\exists a\, :\, \alpha,\; a \in s \land b \in t\left(a\right)\right)\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:46696e7365742e6d656d5f636f65}\paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e6d656d5f636f65}{P17.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:46696e7365742e6d656d5f636f65}\textbf{Membership coercion.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Let \(a\) be an element of \(\alpha\). Let \(s\) be a finite subset of \(\alpha\). Then \(a \in \operatorname{underlying\,set}(s) \iff a \in s\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:46696e7365742e6d656d5f6d6170}\paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e6d656d5f6d6170}{P18.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:46696e7365742e6d656d5f6d6170}\textbf{Membership map.} Let \(\alpha\) and \(\beta\) be types. Let \(f\) be an element of \(\operatorname{Embedding}\left(\alpha, \beta\right)\). Let \(s\) be a finite subset of \(\alpha\). Let \(b\) be an element of \(\beta\). Then \(b \in \operatorname{map}(f,\allowbreak{} s) \iff (\exists a\, :\, \alpha,\; a \in s \land \operatorname{coe}(f,\allowbreak{} a) = b)\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:46696e7365742e6d656d5f70726f64756374}\paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e6d656d5f70726f64756374}{P19.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:46696e7365742e6d656d5f70726f64756374}\textbf{Membership product.} Let \(\alpha\) and \(\beta\) be types. Let \(s\) be a finite subset of \(\alpha\). Let \(t\) be a finite subset of \(\beta\). Let \(p\) be an element of \(\alpha \times \beta\). Then \(p \in \operatorname{sprod}\left(s, t\right) \iff \pi_1(p) \in s \land \pi_2(p) \in t\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:46696e7365742e6d656d5f73696e676c65746f6e}\paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e6d656d5f73696e676c65746f6e}{P20.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:46696e7365742e6d656d5f73696e676c65746f6e}\textbf{Membership singleton.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Let \(a\) and \(b\) be elements of \(\alpha\). Then \(b \in \{a\} \iff b = a\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:46696e7365742e73756d5f636f6e6772}\paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e73756d5f636f6e6772}{P21.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:46696e7365742e73756d5f636f6e6772}\textbf{Equality of sums with equal summands.} Let \(\iota\) and \(M\) be types. Let \(s_{1}\) and \(s_{2}\) be finite subsets of \(\iota\). Equip \(M\) with a commutative additive monoid structure. Let \(f\) and \(g\) be functions of type \(\iota \to M\). Assume \(s_{1} = s_{2}\). Assume \(\forall x\, :\, \iota,\; x \in s_{2} \implies f\left(x\right) = g\left(x\right)\). Then \(\sum_{i\in s_{1}}\left(f(i)\right) = \sum_{i\in s_{2}}\left(g(i)\right)\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:46696e7365742e73756d5f636f6e7374}\paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e73756d5f636f6e7374}{P22.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:46696e7365742e73756d5f636f6e7374}\textbf{The sum of a constant.} Let \(\iota\) and \(M\) be types. Let \(s\) be a finite subset of \(\iota\). Equip \(M\) with a commutative additive monoid structure. Let \(b\) be an element of \(M\). Then \(\sum_{x\in s}\left(b\right) = \sum_{k=1}^{\left|s\right|}\left(b\right)\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:46696e7365742e73756d5f72616e6765}\paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e73756d5f72616e6765}{P23.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:46696e7365742e73756d5f72616e6765}\textbf{Sum range.} Let \(M\) be a type. Equip \(M\) with a commutative additive monoid structure. Let \(n\) be a natural number. Let \(f\) be a function of type \(\mathbb{N} \to M\). Then \(\sum_{0\le i<n}(f(i)) = \sum_{i\in \operatorname{Fin}(n)}(f(\operatorname{val}(i)))\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:46696e7365742e73756d5f72616e67655f737562}\paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e73756d5f72616e67655f737562}{P24.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:46696e7365742e73756d5f72616e67655f737562}\textbf{Sum range difference.} Let \(G\) be a type. Equip \(G\) with an abelian group structure. Let \(f\) be a function of type \(\mathbb{N} \to G\). Let \(n\) be a natural number. Then \(\sum_{0\le i<n}\left(f\left(i + 1\right) - f\left(i\right)\right) = f\left(n\right) - f\left(0\right)\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:46696e7365742e756e69765f756e69717565}\paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e756e69765f756e69717565}{P25.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:46696e7365742e756e69765f756e69717565}\textbf{Whole space unique.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Fix a finite enumeration of \(\alpha\). Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}_{1}\) of type \(\operatorname{Unique}\left(\alpha\right)\). Then \(\alpha = \{\operatorname{default}\}\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:47726f7570576974685a65726f2e746f4d756c44697643616e63656c436c617373}\paperlink{premise:47726f7570576974685a65726f2e746f4d756c44697643616e63656c436c617373}{P26.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:47726f7570576974685a65726f2e746f4d756c44697643616e63656c436c617373}\textbf{To product div cancel class.} Let \(G_{0}\) be a type. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Group\,With\,Zero}\left(G_{0}\right)\). Then \(\operatorname{Mul\,Div\,Cancel\,Class}\left(G_{0}\right)\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4966662e6f665f6571}\paperlink{premise:4966662e6f665f6571}{P27.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4966662e6f665f6571}\textbf{Of equals.} Let \(a\) and \(b\) be propositions. Assume \(a = b\). Then \(a \iff b\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:496e742e6164645f6f6e655f6c655f6f665f6c74}\paperlink{premise:496e742e6164645f6f6e655f6c655f6f665f6c74}{P28.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:496e742e6164645f6f6e655f6c655f6f665f6c74}\textbf{Sum one at most of less than.} Let \(a\) and \(b\) be integers. Assume \(a < b\). Then \(a + 1 \le b\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:496e742e636173745f6469765f6f664e61745f636861725a65726f}\paperlink{premise:496e742e636173745f6469765f6f664e61745f636861725a65726f}{P29.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:496e742e636173745f6469765f6f664e61745f636861725a65726f}\textbf{Cast div of nat char zero.} Let \(k\) be a type. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Division\,Ring}\left(k\right)\). Let \(\mathit{inst}_{1}\) be a proof of \(\operatorname{Char\,Zero}\left(k\right)\). Let \(m\) and \(n\) be natural numbers. Assume \(n \mid m\). Then \(\operatorname{Int\mathord{:}cast}(m / n) = \operatorname{cast}_{k}(m) / \operatorname{cast}_{k}(n)\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:496e742e636173745f696e6a}\paperlink{premise:496e742e636173745f696e6a}{P30.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:496e742e636173745f696e6a}\textbf{Cast injectivity.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Add\,Group\,With\,One}\left(\alpha\right)\). Let \(\mathit{inst}_{1}\) be a proof of \(\operatorname{Char\,Zero}\left(\alpha\right)\). Let \(m\) and \(n\) be integers. Then \(\operatorname{Int\mathord{:}cast}\left(m\right) = \operatorname{Int\mathord{:}cast}\left(n\right) \iff m = n\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:496e742e636173745f6e617443617374}\paperlink{premise:496e742e636173745f6e617443617374}{P31.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:496e742e636173745f6e617443617374}\textbf{Cast nat cast.} Let \(R\) be a type. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Add\,Group\,With\,One}\left(R\right)\). Let \(n\) be a natural number. Then \(\operatorname{Int\mathord{:}cast}\left(n\right) = \operatorname{cast}_{R}\left(n\right)\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:496e742e696e7374436861725a65726f}\paperlink{premise:496e742e696e7374436861725a65726f}{P32.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:496e742e696e7374436861725a65726f}\textbf{Inst char zero.} \(\operatorname{Char\,Zero}\left(\mathbb{Z}\right)\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:496e742e6f664e61745f73756363}\paperlink{premise:496e742e6f664e61745f73756363}{P33.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:496e742e6f664e61745f73756363}\textbf{Of nat successor.} Let \(n\) be a natural number. Then \(n + 1 = n + 1\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:496e742e7375625f6e6f6e6e65675f6f665f6c65}\paperlink{premise:496e742e7375625f6e6f6e6e65675f6f665f6c65}{P34.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:496e742e7375625f6e6f6e6e65675f6f665f6c65}\textbf{Difference nonnegative of at most.} Let \(a\) and \(b\) be integers. Assume \(b \le a\). Then \(0 \le a - b\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:497343616e63656c4164642e746f49734c65667443616e63656c416464}\paperlink{premise:497343616e63656c4164642e746f49734c65667443616e63656c416464}{P35.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:497343616e63656c4164642e746f49734c65667443616e63656c416464}\textbf{To is left cancel sum.} Let \(G\) be a type. Let \(\mathit{inst}\) be an element of \(\operatorname{Add}\left(G\right)\). Let \(\mathit{self}\) be a proof of \(\operatorname{Is\,Cancel\,Add}\left(G\right)\). Then \(\operatorname{Is\,Left\,Cancel\,Add}\left(G\right)\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:49734c65667443616e63656c4164642e6164644c6566745374726963744d6f6e6f5f6f665f6164644c6566744d6f6e6f}\paperlink{premise:49734c65667443616e63656c4164642e6164644c6566745374726963744d6f6e6f5f6f665f6164644c6566744d6f6e6f}{P36.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:49734c65667443616e63656c4164642e6164644c6566745374726963744d6f6e6f5f6f665f6164644c6566744d6f6e6f}\textbf{Sum left strict mono of sum left mono.} Let \(N\) be a type. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Add}\left(N\right)\). Let \(\mathit{inst}_{1}\) be a proof of \(\operatorname{Is\,Left\,Cancel\,Add}\left(N\right)\). Equip \(N\) with a partial order. Let \(\mathit{inst}_{3}\) be a proof of \(\operatorname{Add\,Left\,Mono}\left(N\right)\). Then \(\operatorname{Add\,Left\,Strict\,Mono}\left(N\right)\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:49734f72646572656443616e63656c4164644d6f6e6f69642e746f497343616e63656c416464}\paperlink{premise:49734f72646572656443616e63656c4164644d6f6e6f69642e746f497343616e63656c416464}{P37.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:49734f72646572656443616e63656c4164644d6f6e6f69642e746f497343616e63656c416464}\textbf{To is cancel sum.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Equip \(\alpha\) with a commutative additive monoid structure and a partial order. Assume \(\text{translation preserves and reflects order on }\alpha\). Then \(\operatorname{Is\,Cancel\,Add}\left(\alpha\right)\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:49735374726963744f72646572656452696e672e6e6f5a65726f44697669736f7273}\paperlink{premise:49735374726963744f72646572656452696e672e6e6f5a65726f44697669736f7273}{P38.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:49735374726963744f72646572656452696e672e6e6f5a65726f44697669736f7273}\textbf{No zero divisors.} Let \(R\) be a type. Equip \(R\) with a semiring structure. Equip \(R\) with a linear order. Assume \(R\text{ is a strictly ordered semiring}\). Let \(\mathit{inst}_{3}\) be a proof of \(\operatorname{Exists\,Add\,Of\,LE}\left(R\right)\). Then \(\operatorname{No\,Zero\,Divisors}\left(R\right)\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:49735374726963744f72646572656452696e672e746f49734f72646572656443616e63656c4164644d6f6e6f6964}\paperlink{premise:49735374726963744f72646572656452696e672e746f49734f72646572656443616e63656c4164644d6f6e6f6964}{P39.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:49735374726963744f72646572656452696e672e746f49734f72646572656443616e63656c4164644d6f6e6f6964}\textbf{To is ordered cancel sum monoid.} Let \(R\) be a type. Let \(\mathit{inst}\) be an element of \(\operatorname{Semiring}\left(R\right)\). Let \(\mathit{inst}_{1}\) be an element of \(\operatorname{Partial\,Order}\left(R\right)\). Assume \(R\text{ is a strictly ordered semiring}\). Then \(\text{translation preserves and reflects order on }R\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:49735374726963744f72646572656452696e672e746f49734f72646572656452696e67}\paperlink{premise:49735374726963744f72646572656452696e672e746f49734f72646572656452696e67}{P40.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:49735374726963744f72646572656452696e672e746f49734f72646572656452696e67}\textbf{To is ordered ring.} Let \(R\) be a type. Equip \(R\) with a semiring structure. Equip \(R\) with a partial order. Assume \(R\text{ is a strictly ordered semiring}\). Then \(\operatorname{Is\,Ordered\,Ring}\left(R\right)\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:49735374726963744f72646572656452696e672e746f506f734d756c5374726963744d6f6e6f}\paperlink{premise:49735374726963744f72646572656452696e672e746f506f734d756c5374726963744d6f6e6f}{P41.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:49735374726963744f72646572656452696e672e746f506f734d756c5374726963744d6f6e6f}\textbf{To positivity product strict mono.} Let \(R\) be a type. Equip \(R\) with a semiring structure. Equip \(R\) with a partial order. Assume \(R\text{ is a strictly ordered semiring}\). Then \(\operatorname{Pos\,Mul\,Strict\,Mono}\left(R\right)\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4973556e69742e6d756c5f6469765f63616e63656c5f7269676874}\paperlink{premise:4973556e69742e6d756c5f6469765f63616e63656c5f7269676874}{P42.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4973556e69742e6d756c5f6469765f63616e63656c5f7269676874}\textbf{Product div cancel right.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Division\,Monoid}\left(\alpha\right)\). Let \(b\) be an element of \(\alpha\). Assume \(\operatorname{Is\,Unit}\left(b\right)\). Let \(a\) be an element of \(\alpha\). Then \(a \cdot b / b = a\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4c65616e2e4f6d6567612e436f6e73747261696e742e616464496e657175616c6974795f736174}\paperlink{premise:4c65616e2e4f6d6567612e436f6e73747261696e742e616464496e657175616c6974795f736174}{P43.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4c65616e2e4f6d6567612e436f6e73747261696e742e616464496e657175616c6974795f736174}\textbf{Sum inequality sat.} Let \(c\) be an integer. Let \(x\) and \(y\) be elements of \(\operatorname{Coeffs}\). Assume \(c + \operatorname{dot}\left(x, y\right) \ge 0\). Then \(\operatorname{sat^{\prime}}(\operatorname{Lean\mathord{:}Omega\mathord{:}Constraint\mathord{:}mk}(\operatorname{some}(-c),\allowbreak{} \operatorname{none}),\allowbreak{} x,\allowbreak{} y) = \mathrm{true}\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4c65616e2e4f6d6567612e436f6e73747261696e742e636f6d62696e655f73617427}\paperlink{premise:4c65616e2e4f6d6567612e436f6e73747261696e742e636f6d62696e655f73617427}{P44.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4c65616e2e4f6d6567612e436f6e73747261696e742e636f6d62696e655f73617427}\textbf{Combine sat'.} Let \(s\) and \(t\) be elements of \(\operatorname{Constraint}\). Let \(x\) and \(y\) be elements of \(\operatorname{Coeffs}\). Assume \(\operatorname{sat^{\prime}}\left(s, x, y\right) = \mathrm{true}\). Assume \(\operatorname{sat^{\prime}}\left(t, x, y\right) = \mathrm{true}\). Then \(\operatorname{sat^{\prime}}\left(\operatorname{combine}\left(s, t\right), x, y\right) = \mathrm{true}\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4c65616e2e4f6d6567612e436f6e73747261696e742e6e6f745f736174275f6f665f6973496d706f737369626c65}\paperlink{premise:4c65616e2e4f6d6567612e436f6e73747261696e742e6e6f745f736174275f6f665f6973496d706f737369626c65}{P45.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4c65616e2e4f6d6567612e436f6e73747261696e742e6e6f745f736174275f6f665f6973496d706f737369626c65}\textbf{Not sat' of is impossible.} Let \(c\) be an element of \(\operatorname{Constraint}\). Assume \(\operatorname{is\,Impossible}\left(c\right) = \mathrm{true}\). Let \(x\) and \(y\) be elements of \(\operatorname{Coeffs}\). Then \(\neg \left(\operatorname{sat^{\prime}}\left(c, x, y\right) = \mathrm{true}\right)\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4c65616e2e4f6d6567612e496e742e6164645f636f6e6772}\paperlink{premise:4c65616e2e4f6d6567612e496e742e6164645f636f6e6772}{P46.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4c65616e2e4f6d6567612e496e742e6164645f636f6e6772}\textbf{Sum congruence.} Let \(a\), \(b\), \(c\), and \(d\) be integers. Assume \(a = b\). Assume \(c = d\). Then \(a + c = b + d\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4c65616e2e4f6d6567612e496e742e6f664e61745f6c745f6f665f6c74}\paperlink{premise:4c65616e2e4f6d6567612e496e742e6f664e61745f6c745f6f665f6c74}{P47.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4c65616e2e4f6d6567612e496e742e6f664e61745f6c745f6f665f6c74}\textbf{Of nat less than of less than.} Let \(x\) and \(y\) be natural numbers. Assume \(x < y\). Then \(x < y\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4c65616e2e4f6d6567612e496e742e7375625f636f6e6772}\paperlink{premise:4c65616e2e4f6d6567612e496e742e7375625f636f6e6772}{P48.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4c65616e2e4f6d6567612e496e742e7375625f636f6e6772}\textbf{Difference congruence.} Let \(a\), \(b\), \(c\), and \(d\) be integers. Assume \(a = b\). Assume \(c = d\). Then \(a - c = b - d\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4c65616e2e4f6d6567612e4c696e656172436f6d626f2e6164645f6576616c}\paperlink{premise:4c65616e2e4f6d6567612e4c696e656172436f6d626f2e6164645f6576616c}{P49.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4c65616e2e4f6d6567612e4c696e656172436f6d626f2e6164645f6576616c}\textbf{Sum eval.} Let \(l_{1}\) and \(l_{2}\) be elements of \(\operatorname{Linear\,Combo}\). Let \(v\) be an element of \(\operatorname{Coeffs}\). Then \(\operatorname{eval}(l_{1} + l_{2},\allowbreak{} v) = \operatorname{eval}(l_{1},\allowbreak{} v) + \operatorname{eval}(l_{2},\allowbreak{} v)\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4c65616e2e4f6d6567612e4c696e656172436f6d626f2e636f6f7264696e6174655f6576616c5f30}\paperlink{premise:4c65616e2e4f6d6567612e4c696e656172436f6d626f2e636f6f7264696e6174655f6576616c5f30}{P50.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4c65616e2e4f6d6567612e4c696e656172436f6d626f2e636f6f7264696e6174655f6576616c5f30}\textbf{Coordinate eval 0.} Let \(\mathit{a0}\) be an integer. Let \(t\) be a list of elements of \(\mathbb{Z}\). Then \(\operatorname{eval}(\operatorname{coordinate}(0),\allowbreak{} \operatorname{of\,List}(\langle \mathit{a0}\rangle \mathbin{\Vert} t)) = \mathit{a0}\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4c65616e2e4f6d6567612e4c696e656172436f6d626f2e636f6f7264696e6174655f6576616c5f31}\paperlink{premise:4c65616e2e4f6d6567612e4c696e656172436f6d626f2e636f6f7264696e6174655f6576616c5f31}{P51.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4c65616e2e4f6d6567612e4c696e656172436f6d626f2e636f6f7264696e6174655f6576616c5f31}\textbf{Coordinate eval 1.} Let \(\mathit{a0}\) and \(\mathit{a1}\) be integers. Let \(t\) be a list of elements of \(\mathbb{Z}\). Then \(\operatorname{eval}(\operatorname{coordinate}(1),\allowbreak{} \operatorname{of\,List}(\langle \mathit{a0}\rangle \mathbin{\Vert} (\langle \mathit{a1}\rangle \mathbin{\Vert} t))) = \mathit{a1}\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4c65616e2e4f6d6567612e4c696e656172436f6d626f2e7375625f6576616c}\paperlink{premise:4c65616e2e4f6d6567612e4c696e656172436f6d626f2e7375625f6576616c}{P52.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4c65616e2e4f6d6567612e4c696e656172436f6d626f2e7375625f6576616c}\textbf{Difference eval.} Let \(l_{1}\) and \(l_{2}\) be elements of \(\operatorname{Linear\,Combo}\). Let \(v\) be an element of \(\operatorname{Coeffs}\). Then \(\operatorname{eval}(l_{1} - l_{2},\allowbreak{} v) = \operatorname{eval}(l_{1},\allowbreak{} v) - \operatorname{eval}(l_{2},\allowbreak{} v)\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4c65616e2e4f6d6567612e746964795f736174}\paperlink{premise:4c65616e2e4f6d6567612e746964795f736174}{P53.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4c65616e2e4f6d6567612e746964795f736174}\textbf{Tidy sat.} Let \(s\) be an element of \(\operatorname{Constraint}\). Let \(x\) and \(v\) be elements of \(\operatorname{Coeffs}\). Assume \(\operatorname{sat^{\prime}}\left(s, x, v\right) = \mathrm{true}\). Then \(\operatorname{sat^{\prime}}(\operatorname{tidy\,Constraint}(s,\allowbreak{} x),\allowbreak{} \operatorname{tidy\,Coeffs}(s,\allowbreak{} x),\allowbreak{} v) = \mathrm{true}\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4d6174686c69622e4d6574612e4e6f726d4e756d2e4973496e742e6f665f726177}\paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e4d6574612e4e6f726d4e756d2e4973496e742e6f665f726177}{P54.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e4d6574612e4e6f726d4e756d2e4973496e742e6f665f726177}\textbf{Of raw.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Ring}\left(\alpha\right)\). Let \(n\) be an integer. Then \(\operatorname{Is\,Int}\left(\operatorname{Int\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}\left(n\right), n\right)\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4d6174686c69622e4d6574612e4e6f726d4e756d2e4973496e742e746f5f69734e6174}\paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e4d6574612e4e6f726d4e756d2e4973496e742e746f5f69734e6174}{P55.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e4d6574612e4e6f726d4e756d2e4973496e742e746f5f69734e6174}\textbf{To is nat.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Ring}\left(\alpha\right)\). Let \(a\) be an element of \(\alpha\). Let \(n\) be a natural number. Assume \(\operatorname{Is\,Int}\left(a, n\right)\). Then \(\operatorname{Is\,Nat}\left(a, n\right)\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4d6174686c69622e4d6574612e4e6f726d4e756d2e4973496e742e746f5f7261775f6571}\paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e4d6574612e4e6f726d4e756d2e4973496e742e746f5f7261775f6571}{P56.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e4d6574612e4e6f726d4e756d2e4973496e742e746f5f7261775f6571}\textbf{To raw equals.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Let \(a\) be an element of \(\alpha\). Let \(n\) be an integer. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Ring}\left(\alpha\right)\). Assume \(\operatorname{Is\,Int}\left(a, n\right)\). Then \(a = \operatorname{Int\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}\left(n\right)\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4d6174686c69622e4d6574612e4e6f726d4e756d2e49734e61742e6f665f726177}\paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e4d6574612e4e6f726d4e756d2e49734e61742e6f665f726177}{P57.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e4d6574612e4e6f726d4e756d2e49734e61742e6f665f726177}\textbf{Of raw.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Add\,Monoid\,With\,One}\left(\alpha\right)\). Let \(n\) be a natural number. Then \(\operatorname{Is\,Nat}\left(\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}\left(n\right), n\right)\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4d6174686c69622e4d6574612e4e6f726d4e756d2e49734e61742e746f5f6973496e74}\paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e4d6574612e4e6f726d4e756d2e49734e61742e746f5f6973496e74}{P58.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e4d6574612e4e6f726d4e756d2e49734e61742e746f5f6973496e74}\textbf{To is int.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Ring}\left(\alpha\right)\). Let \(a\) be an element of \(\alpha\). Let \(n\) be a natural number. Assume \(\operatorname{Is\,Nat}\left(a, n\right)\). Then \(\operatorname{Is\,Int}\left(a, n\right)\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4d6174686c69622e4d6574612e4e6f726d4e756d2e49734e61742e746f5f7261775f6571}\paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e4d6574612e4e6f726d4e756d2e49734e61742e746f5f7261775f6571}{P59.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e4d6574612e4e6f726d4e756d2e49734e61742e746f5f7261775f6571}\textbf{To raw equals.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Let \(a\) be an element of \(\alpha\). Let \(n\) be a natural number. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Add\,Monoid\,With\,One}\left(\alpha\right)\). Assume \(\operatorname{Is\,Nat}\left(a, n\right)\). Then \(a = \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}\left(n\right)\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4d6174686c69622e4d6574612e4e6f726d4e756d2e6973496e745f616464}\paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e4d6574612e4e6f726d4e756d2e6973496e745f616464}{P60.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e4d6574612e4e6f726d4e756d2e6973496e745f616464}\textbf{Is int sum.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Ring}\left(\alpha\right)\). Let \(f\) be a function of type \(\alpha \to \alpha \to \alpha\). Let \(a\) and \(b\) be elements of \(\alpha\). Let \(a^{'}\), \(b^{'}\), and \(c\) be integers. Assume \(f = \left(x \mapsto y \mapsto x + y\right)\). Assume \(\operatorname{Is\,Int}\left(a, a^{'}\right)\). Assume \(\operatorname{Is\,Int}\left(b, b^{'}\right)\). Assume \(a^{'} + b^{'} = c\). Then \(\operatorname{Is\,Int}\left(f\left(a, b\right), c\right)\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4d6174686c69622e4d6574612e4e6f726d4e756d2e6973496e745f6d756c}\paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e4d6574612e4e6f726d4e756d2e6973496e745f6d756c}{P61.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e4d6574612e4e6f726d4e756d2e6973496e745f6d756c}\textbf{Is int product.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Ring}\left(\alpha\right)\). Let \(f\) be a function of type \(\alpha \to \alpha \to \alpha\). Let \(a\) and \(b\) be elements of \(\alpha\). Let \(a^{'}\), \(b^{'}\), and \(c\) be integers. Assume \(f = \left(x \mapsto y \mapsto x \cdot y\right)\). Assume \(\operatorname{Is\,Int}\left(a, a^{'}\right)\). Assume \(\operatorname{Is\,Int}\left(b, b^{'}\right)\). Assume \(a^{'} \cdot b^{'} = c\). Then \(\operatorname{Is\,Int}\left(a \cdot b, c\right)\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4d6174686c69622e4d6574612e4e6f726d4e756d2e69734e61745f616464}\paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e4d6574612e4e6f726d4e756d2e69734e61745f616464}{P62.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e4d6574612e4e6f726d4e756d2e69734e61745f616464}\textbf{Is nat sum.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Add\,Monoid\,With\,One}\left(\alpha\right)\). Let \(f\) be a function of type \(\alpha \to \alpha \to \alpha\). Let \(a\) and \(b\) be elements of \(\alpha\). Let \(a^{'}\), \(b^{'}\), and \(c\) be natural numbers. Assume \(f = \left(x \mapsto y \mapsto x + y\right)\). Assume \(\operatorname{Is\,Nat}\left(a, a^{'}\right)\). Assume \(\operatorname{Is\,Nat}\left(b, b^{'}\right)\). Assume \(a^{'} + b^{'} = c\). Then \(\operatorname{Is\,Nat}\left(f\left(a, b\right), c\right)\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4d6174686c69622e4d6574612e4e6f726d4e756d2e69734e61745f6d756c}\paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e4d6574612e4e6f726d4e756d2e69734e61745f6d756c}{P63.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e4d6574612e4e6f726d4e756d2e69734e61745f6d756c}\textbf{Is nat product.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Equip \(\alpha\) with a semiring structure. Let \(f\) be a function of type \(\alpha \to \alpha \to \alpha\). Let \(a\) and \(b\) be elements of \(\alpha\). Let \(a^{'}\), \(b^{'}\), and \(c\) be natural numbers. Assume \(f = \left(x \mapsto y \mapsto x \cdot y\right)\). Assume \(\operatorname{Is\,Nat}\left(a, a^{'}\right)\). Assume \(\operatorname{Is\,Nat}\left(b, b^{'}\right)\). Assume \(a^{'} \cdot b^{'} = c\). Then \(\operatorname{Is\,Nat}\left(a \cdot b, c\right)\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4d6174686c69622e4d6574612e4e6f726d4e756d2e69734e61745f6f664e6174}\paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e4d6574612e4e6f726d4e756d2e69734e61745f6f664e6174}{P64.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e4d6574612e4e6f726d4e756d2e69734e61745f6f664e6174}\textbf{Is nat of nat.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Add\,Monoid\,With\,One}\left(\alpha\right)\). Let \(a\) be an element of \(\alpha\). Let \(n\) be a natural number. Assume \(\operatorname{cast}_{\alpha}\left(n\right) = a\). Then \(\operatorname{Is\,Nat}\left(a, n\right)\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4d6174686c69622e4d6574612e506f73697469766974792e706f735f6f665f69734e6174}\paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e4d6574612e506f73697469766974792e706f735f6f665f69734e6174}{P65.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e4d6574612e506f73697469766974792e706f735f6f665f69734e6174}\textbf{Positivity of is nat.} Let \(A\) be a type. Let \(e\) be an element of \(A\). Let \(n\) be a natural number. Equip \(A\) with a semiring structure. Equip \(A\) with a partial order. Let \(\mathit{inst}_{2}\) be a proof of \(\operatorname{Is\,Ordered\,Ring}\left(A\right)\). Let \(\mathit{inst}_{3}\) be a proof of \(\operatorname{Nontrivial}\left(A\right)\). Assume \(\operatorname{Is\,Nat}\left(e, n\right)\). Assume \(\operatorname{ble}\left(1, n\right) = \mathrm{true}\). Then \(0 < e\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6164645f636f6e6772}\paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6164645f636f6e6772}{P66.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6164645f636f6e6772}\textbf{Sum congruence.} Let \(R\) be a type. Equip \(R\) with a commutative semiring structure. Let \(a\), \(a^{'}\), \(b\), \(b^{'}\), and \(c\) be elements of \(R\). Assume \(a = a^{'}\). Assume \(b = b^{'}\). Assume \(a^{'} + b^{'} = c\). Then \(a + b = c\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6164645f6d756c}\paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6164645f6d756c}{P67.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6164645f6d756c}\textbf{Sum product.} Let \(R\) be a type. Equip \(R\) with a commutative semiring structure. Let \(a_{1}\), \(a_{2}\), \(b\), \(c_{1}\), \(c_{2}\), and \(d\) be elements of \(R\). Assume \(a_{1} \cdot b = c_{1}\). Assume \(a_{2} \cdot b = c_{2}\). Assume \(c_{1} + c_{2} = d\). Then \(\left(a_{1} + a_{2}\right) \cdot b = d\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6164645f6f7665726c61705f7066}\paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6164645f6f7665726c61705f7066}{P68.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6164645f6f7665726c61705f7066}\textbf{Sum overlap pf.} Let \(R\) be a type. Equip \(R\) with a commutative semiring structure. Let \(a\), \(b\), \(c\), and \(x\) be elements of \(R\). Let \(e\) be a natural number. Assume \(a + b = c\). Then \(x^{e} \cdot a + x^{e} \cdot b = x^{e} \cdot c\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6164645f70665f6164645f6774}\paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6164645f70665f6164645f6774}{P69.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6164645f70665f6164645f6774}\textbf{Sum pf sum greater than.} Let \(R\) be a type. Equip \(R\) with a commutative semiring structure. Let \(a\), \(b_{2}\), \(c\), and \(b_{1}\) be elements of \(R\). Assume \(a + b_{2} = c\). Then \(a + \left(b_{1} + b_{2}\right) = b_{1} + c\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6164645f70665f6164645f6c74}\paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6164645f70665f6164645f6c74}{P70.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6164645f70665f6164645f6c74}\textbf{Sum pf sum less than.} Let \(R\) be a type. Equip \(R\) with a commutative semiring structure. Let \(a_{2}\), \(b\), \(c\), and \(a_{1}\) be elements of \(R\). Assume \(a_{2} + b = c\). Then \(a_{1} + a_{2} + b = a_{1} + c\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6164645f70665f6164645f6f7665726c6170}\paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6164645f70665f6164645f6f7665726c6170}{P71.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6164645f70665f6164645f6f7665726c6170}\textbf{Sum pf sum overlap.} Let \(R\) be a type. Equip \(R\) with a commutative semiring structure. Let \(a_{1}\), \(a_{2}\), \(b_{1}\), \(b_{2}\), \(c_{1}\), and \(c_{2}\) be elements of \(R\). Assume \(a_{1} + b_{1} = c_{1}\). Assume \(a_{2} + b_{2} = c_{2}\). Then \(a_{1} + a_{2} + \left(b_{1} + b_{2}\right) = c_{1} + c_{2}\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6164645f70665f6164645f7a65726f}\paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6164645f70665f6164645f7a65726f}{P72.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6164645f70665f6164645f7a65726f}\textbf{Sum pf sum zero.} Let \(R\) be a type. Equip \(R\) with a commutative semiring structure. Let \(a\) be an element of \(R\). Then \(a + 0 = a\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6164645f70665f7a65726f5f616464}\paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6164645f70665f7a65726f5f616464}{P73.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6164645f70665f7a65726f5f616464}\textbf{Sum pf zero sum.} Let \(R\) be a type. Equip \(R\) with a commutative semiring structure. Let \(b\) be an element of \(R\). Then \(0 + b = b\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e61746f6d5f7066}\paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e61746f6d5f7066}{P74.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e61746f6d5f7066}\textbf{Atom pf.} Let \(R\) be a type. Equip \(R\) with a commutative semiring structure. Let \(a\) be an element of \(R\). Then \(a = a^{\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}(1) + 0\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e636173745f706f73}\paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e636173745f706f73}{P75.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e636173745f706f73}\textbf{Cast positivity.} Let \(R\) be a type. Equip \(R\) with a commutative semiring structure. Let \(a\) be an element of \(R\). Let \(n\) be a natural number. Assume \(\operatorname{Is\,Nat}\left(a, n\right)\). Then \(a = \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}\left(n\right) + 0\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6d756c5f616464}\paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6d756c5f616464}{P76.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6d756c5f616464}\textbf{Product sum.} Let \(R\) be a type. Equip \(R\) with a commutative semiring structure. Let \(a\), \(b_{1}\), \(b_{2}\), \(c_{1}\), \(c_{2}\), and \(d\) be elements of \(R\). Assume \(a \cdot b_{1} = c_{1}\). Assume \(a \cdot b_{2} = c_{2}\). Assume \(c_{1} + 0 + c_{2} = d\). Then \(a \cdot \left(b_{1} + b_{2}\right) = d\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6d756c5f636f6e6772}\paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6d756c5f636f6e6772}{P77.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6d756c5f636f6e6772}\textbf{Product congruence.} Let \(R\) be a type. Equip \(R\) with a commutative semiring structure. Let \(a\), \(a^{'}\), \(b\), \(b^{'}\), and \(c\) be elements of \(R\). Assume \(a = a^{'}\). Assume \(b = b^{'}\). Assume \(a^{'} \cdot b^{'} = c\). Then \(a \cdot b = c\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6d756c5f6f6e65}\paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6d756c5f6f6e65}{P78.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6d756c5f6f6e65}\textbf{Product one.} Let \(R\) be a type. Equip \(R\) with a commutative semiring structure. Let \(a\) be an element of \(R\). Then \(a \cdot \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}\left(1\right) = a\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6d756c5f70665f6c656674}\paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6d756c5f70665f6c656674}{P79.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6d756c5f70665f6c656674}\textbf{Product pf left.} Let \(R\) be a type. Equip \(R\) with a commutative semiring structure. Let \(a_{3}\), \(b\), \(c\), and \(a_{1}\) be elements of \(R\). Let \(a_{2}\) be a natural number. Assume \(a_{3} \cdot b = c\). Then \(a_{1}^{a_{2}} \cdot a_{3} \cdot b = a_{1}^{a_{2}} \cdot c\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6d756c5f70665f7269676874}\paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6d756c5f70665f7269676874}{P80.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6d756c5f70665f7269676874}\textbf{Product pf right.} Let \(R\) be a type. Equip \(R\) with a commutative semiring structure. Let \(a\), \(b_{3}\), \(c\), and \(b_{1}\) be elements of \(R\). Let \(b_{2}\) be a natural number. Assume \(a \cdot b_{3} = c\). Then \(a \cdot \left(b_{1}^{b_{2}} \cdot b_{3}\right) = b_{1}^{b_{2}} \cdot c\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6d756c5f70705f70665f6f7665726c6170}\paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6d756c5f70705f70665f6f7665726c6170}{P81.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6d756c5f70705f70665f6f7665726c6170}\textbf{Product pp pf overlap.} Let \(R\) be a type. Equip \(R\) with a commutative semiring structure. Let \(a_{2}\), \(b_{2}\), and \(c\) be elements of \(R\). Let \(\mathit{ea}\), \(\mathit{eb}\), and \(e\) be natural numbers. Let \(x\) be an element of \(R\). Assume \(\mathit{ea} + \mathit{eb} = e\). Assume \(a_{2} \cdot b_{2} = c\). Then \(x^{\mathit{ea}} \cdot a_{2} \cdot \left(x^{\mathit{eb}} \cdot b_{2}\right) = x^{e} \cdot c\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6d756c5f7a65726f}\paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6d756c5f7a65726f}{P82.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6d756c5f7a65726f}\textbf{Product zero.} Let \(R\) be a type. Equip \(R\) with a commutative semiring structure. Let \(a\) be an element of \(R\). Then \(a \cdot 0 = 0\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6e65675f616464}\paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6e65675f616464}{P83.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6e65675f616464}\textbf{Neg sum.} Let \(R\) be a type. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Ring}\left(R\right)\). Let \(a_{1}\), \(a_{2}\), \(b_{1}\), and \(b_{2}\) be elements of \(R\). Assume \(-a_{1} = b_{1}\). Assume \(-a_{2} = b_{2}\). Then \(-\left(a_{1} + a_{2}\right) = b_{1} + b_{2}\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6e65675f636f6e6772}\paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6e65675f636f6e6772}{P84.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6e65675f636f6e6772}\textbf{Neg congruence.} Let \(R\) be a type. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Ring}\left(R\right)\). Let \(a\), \(a^{'}\), and \(b\) be elements of \(R\). Assume \(a = a^{'}\). Assume \(-a^{'} = b\). Then \(-a = b\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6e65675f6d756c}\paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6e65675f6d756c}{P85.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6e65675f6d756c}\textbf{Neg product.} Let \(R\) be a type. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Ring}\left(R\right)\). Let \(a_{1}\) be an element of \(R\). Let \(a_{2}\) be a natural number. Let \(a_{3}\) and \(b\) be elements of \(R\). Assume \(-a_{3} = b\). Then \(-\left(a_{1}^{a_{2}} \cdot a_{3}\right) = a_{1}^{a_{2}} \cdot b\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6e65675f6f6e655f6d756c}\paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6e65675f6f6e655f6d756c}{P86.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6e65675f6f6e655f6d756c}\textbf{Neg one product.} Let \(R\) be a type. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Ring}\left(R\right)\). Let \(a\) and \(b\) be elements of \(R\). Assume \(\operatorname{Int\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}\left(\operatorname{neg\,Of\,Nat}\left(1\right)\right) \cdot a = b\). Then \(-a = b\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6e65675f7a65726f}\paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6e65675f7a65726f}{P87.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6e65675f7a65726f}\textbf{Neg zero.} Let \(R\) be a type. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Ring}\left(R\right)\). Then \(-0 = 0\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6f665f6571}\paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6f665f6571}{P88.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6f665f6571}\textbf{Of equals.} Let \(\alpha\) be a sort. Let \(a\), \(b\), and \(c\) be elements of \(\alpha\). Assume \(a = c\). Assume \(b = c\). Then \(a = b\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6f6e655f6d756c}\paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6f6e655f6d756c}{P89.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6f6e655f6d756c}\textbf{One product.} Let \(R\) be a type. Equip \(R\) with a commutative semiring structure. Let \(a\) be an element of \(R\). Then \(\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}\left(1\right) \cdot a = a\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e7375625f636f6e6772}\paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e7375625f636f6e6772}{P90.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e7375625f636f6e6772}\textbf{Difference congruence.} Let \(R\) be a type. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Ring}\left(R\right)\). Let \(a\), \(a^{'}\), \(b\), \(b^{'}\), and \(c\) be elements of \(R\). Assume \(a = a^{'}\). Assume \(b = b^{'}\). Assume \(a^{'} - b^{'} = c\). Then \(a - b = c\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e7375625f7066}\paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e7375625f7066}{P91.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e7375625f7066}\textbf{Difference pf.} Let \(R\) be a type. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Ring}\left(R\right)\). Let \(a\), \(b\), \(c\), and \(d\) be elements of \(R\). Assume \(-b = c\). Assume \(a + c = d\). Then \(a - b = d\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e7a65726f5f6d756c}\paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e7a65726f5f6d756c}{P92.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e7a65726f5f6d756c}\textbf{Zero product.} Let \(R\) be a type. Equip \(R\) with a commutative semiring structure. Let \(b\) be an element of \(R\). Then \(0 \cdot b = 0\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4d756c5a65726f436c6173732e6d756c5f7a65726f}\paperlink{premise:4d756c5a65726f436c6173732e6d756c5f7a65726f}{P93.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d756c5a65726f436c6173732e6d756c5f7a65726f}\textbf{Product zero.} Let \(M_{0}\) be a type. Fix a structure \(\mathit{self}\) of type \(\operatorname{Mul\,Zero\,Class}\left(M_{0}\right)\). Let \(a\) be an element of \(M_{0}\). Then \(a \cdot 0 = 0\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4e61742e53696d70726f632e6164645f7375625f6164645f6c65}\paperlink{premise:4e61742e53696d70726f632e6164645f7375625f6164645f6c65}{P94.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4e61742e53696d70726f632e6164645f7375625f6164645f6c65}\textbf{Sum difference sum at most.} Let \(a\), \(c\), \(b\), and \(d\) be natural numbers. Assume \(b \le d\). Then \(a + b - \left(c + d\right) = a - \left(c + \left(d - b\right)\right)\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4e61742e6164645f636f6d6d}\paperlink{premise:4e61742e6164645f636f6d6d}{P95.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4e61742e6164645f636f6d6d}\textbf{Addition commutativity.} Let \(n\) and \(m\) be natural numbers. Then \(n + m = m + n\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4e61742e6164645f65715f7a65726f}\paperlink{premise:4e61742e6164645f65715f7a65726f}{P96.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4e61742e6164645f65715f7a65726f}\textbf{Sum equals zero.} Let \(m\) and \(n\) be natural numbers. Then \(m + n = 0 \iff m = 0 \land n = 0\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4e61742e6164645f6c6566745f696e6a}\paperlink{premise:4e61742e6164645f6c6566745f696e6a}{P97.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4e61742e6164645f6c6566745f696e6a}\textbf{Alias.} Let \(m\), \(k\) and \(n\) be natural numbers. Then \(m + n = k + n \iff m = k\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4e61742e6164645f72696768745f696e6a}\paperlink{premise:4e61742e6164645f72696768745f696e6a}{P98.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4e61742e6164645f72696768745f696e6a}\textbf{Alias.} Let \(m\), \(k\) and \(n\) be natural numbers. Then \(n + m = n + k \iff m = k\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4e61742e6164645f7375625f6173736f63}\paperlink{premise:4e61742e6164645f7375625f6173736f63}{P99.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4e61742e6164645f7375625f6173736f63}\textbf{Sum difference assoc.} Let \(m\) and \(k\) be natural numbers. Assume \(k \le m\). Let \(n\) be a natural number. Then \(n + m - k = n + \left(m - k\right)\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4e61742e636173745f616464}\paperlink{premise:4e61742e636173745f616464}{P100.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4e61742e636173745f616464}\textbf{Cast sum.} Let \(R\) be a type. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Add\,Monoid\,With\,One}\left(R\right)\). Let \(m\) and \(n\) be natural numbers. Then \(\operatorname{cast}_{R}(m + n) = \operatorname{cast}_{R}(m) + \operatorname{cast}_{R}(n)\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4e61742e636173745f696e6a}\paperlink{premise:4e61742e636173745f696e6a}{P101.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4e61742e636173745f696e6a}\textbf{Cast injectivity.} Let \(R\) be a type. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Add\,Monoid\,With\,One}\left(R\right)\). Let \(\mathit{inst}_{1}\) be a proof of \(\operatorname{Char\,Zero}\left(R\right)\). Let \(m\) and \(n\) be natural numbers. Then \(\operatorname{cast}_{R}\left(m\right) = \operatorname{cast}_{R}\left(n\right) \iff m = n\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4e61742e636173745f6d756c}\paperlink{premise:4e61742e636173745f6d756c}{P102.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4e61742e636173745f6d756c}\textbf{Cast product.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Non\,Assoc\,Semiring}\left(\alpha\right)\). Let \(m\) and \(n\) be natural numbers. Then \(\operatorname{cast}_{\alpha}(m \cdot n) = \operatorname{cast}_{\alpha}(m) \cdot \operatorname{cast}_{\alpha}(n)\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4e61742e636173745f6e6f6e6e6567}\paperlink{premise:4e61742e636173745f6e6f6e6e6567}{P103.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4e61742e636173745f6e6f6e6e6567}\textbf{Cast nonnegative.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Equip \(\alpha\) with a semiring structure. Equip \(\alpha\) with a partial order. Let \(\mathit{inst}_{2}\) be a proof of \(\operatorname{Is\,Ordered\,Ring}\left(\alpha\right)\). Let \(n\) be a natural number. Then \(0 \le \operatorname{cast}_{\alpha}\left(n\right)\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4e61742e636173745f6f6e65}\paperlink{premise:4e61742e636173745f6f6e65}{P104.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4e61742e636173745f6f6e65}\textbf{Cast one.} Let \(R\) be a type. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Add\,Monoid\,With\,One}\left(R\right)\). Then \(\operatorname{cast}_{R}\left(1\right) = 1\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4e61742e636173745f737562}\paperlink{premise:4e61742e636173745f737562}{P105.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4e61742e636173745f737562}\textbf{Cast difference.} Let \(R\) be a type. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Add\,Group\,With\,One}\left(R\right)\). Let \(m\) and \(n\) be natural numbers. Assume \(m \le n\). Then \(\operatorname{cast}_{R}(n - m) = \operatorname{cast}_{R}(n) - \operatorname{cast}_{R}(m)\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4e61742e636173745f73756d}\paperlink{premise:4e61742e636173745f73756d}{P106.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4e61742e636173745f73756d}\textbf{Cast sum.} Let \(\alpha\) and \(\beta\) be types. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Add\,Comm\,Monoid\,With\,One}\left(\beta\right)\). Let \(s\) be a finite subset of \(\alpha\). Let \(f\) be a function of type \(\alpha \to \mathbb{N}\). Then \(\operatorname{cast}_{\beta}(\sum_{x\in s}(f(x))) = \sum_{x\in s}(\operatorname{cast}_{\beta}(f(x)))\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4e61742e636173745f7a65726f}\paperlink{premise:4e61742e636173745f7a65726f}{P107.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4e61742e636173745f7a65726f}\textbf{Cast zero.} Let \(R\) be a type. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Add\,Monoid\,With\,One}\left(R\right)\). Then \(\operatorname{cast}_{R}\left(0\right) = 0\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4e61742e63656e7472616c42696e6f6d5f7a65726f}\paperlink{premise:4e61742e63656e7472616c42696e6f6d5f7a65726f}{P108.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4e61742e63656e7472616c42696e6f6d5f7a65726f}\textbf{Central binom zero.} \(\binom{2 \cdot 0}{0} = 1\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4e61742e65715f6d756c5f6f665f6469765f65715f7269676874}\paperlink{premise:4e61742e65715f6d756c5f6f665f6469765f65715f7269676874}{P109.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4e61742e65715f6d756c5f6f665f6469765f65715f7269676874}\textbf{Equals product of div equals right.} Let \(a\), \(b\), and \(c\) be natural numbers. Assume \(b \mid a\). Assume \(a / b = c\). Then \(a = b \cdot c\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4e61742e696e737443616e6f6e6963616c6c794f726465726564416464}\paperlink{premise:4e61742e696e737443616e6f6e6963616c6c794f726465726564416464}{P110.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4e61742e696e737443616e6f6e6963616c6c794f726465726564416464}\textbf{Inst canonically ordered sum.} \(\operatorname{Canonically\,Ordered\,Add}\left(\mathbb{N}\right)\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4e61742e696e73744e655a65726f53756363}\paperlink{premise:4e61742e696e73744e655a65726f53756363}{P111.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4e61742e696e73744e655a65726f53756363}\textbf{Inst differs from zero successor.} Let \(n\) be a natural number. Then \(\operatorname{Ne\,Zero}\left(n + 1\right)\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4e61742e696e73744f726465726564537562}\paperlink{premise:4e61742e696e73744f726465726564537562}{P112.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4e61742e696e73744f726465726564537562}\textbf{Inst ordered difference.} \(\operatorname{Ordered\,Sub}\left(\mathbb{N}\right)\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4e61742e6c745f6f665f6e6f745f6c65}\paperlink{premise:4e61742e6c745f6f665f6e6f745f6c65}{P113.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4e61742e6c745f6f665f6e6f745f6c65}\textbf{Less than of not at most.} Let \(a\) and \(b\) be natural numbers. Assume \(\neg \left(a \le b\right)\). Then \(b < a\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4e61742e6e6f436f6e667573696f6e}\paperlink{premise:4e61742e6e6f436f6e667573696f6e}{P114.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4e61742e6e6f436f6e667573696f6e}\textbf{No confusion.} Let \(P\) be a proposition. Let \(\mathit{v1}\) and \(\mathit{v2}\) be natural numbers. Assume \(\mathit{v1} = \mathit{v2}\). Then \(\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}no\,Confusion\,Type}\left(P, \mathit{v1}, \mathit{v2}\right)\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4e61742e7375625f73656c66}\paperlink{premise:4e61742e7375625f73656c66}{P115.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4e61742e7375625f73656c66}\textbf{Difference self.} Let \(n\) be a natural number. Then \(n - n = 0\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4e61742e737563635f6476645f63656e7472616c42696e6f6d}\paperlink{premise:4e61742e737563635f6476645f63656e7472616c42696e6f6d}{P116.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4e61742e737563635f6476645f63656e7472616c42696e6f6d}\textbf{Successor divisibility central binom.} Let \(n\) be a natural number. Then \(n + 1 \mid \binom{2 \cdot n}{n}\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4e61742e737563635f65715f6164645f6f6e65}\paperlink{premise:4e61742e737563635f65715f6164645f6f6e65}{P117.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4e61742e737563635f65715f6164645f6f6e65}\textbf{Successor equals sum one.} Let \(n\) be a natural number. Then \(n + 1 = n + 1\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4e61742e737563635f6d756c5f63656e7472616c42696e6f6d5f73756363}\paperlink{premise:4e61742e737563635f6d756c5f63656e7472616c42696e6f6d5f73756363}{P118.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4e61742e737563635f6d756c5f63656e7472616c42696e6f6d5f73756363}\textbf{Successor product central binom successor.} Let \(n\) be a natural number. Then \((n + 1) \cdot \binom{2 \cdot (n + 1)}{n + 1} = 2 \cdot (2 \cdot n + 1) \cdot \binom{2 \cdot n}{n}\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4e655a65726f2e636861725a65726f5f6f6e65}\paperlink{premise:4e655a65726f2e636861725a65726f5f6f6e65}{P119.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4e655a65726f2e636861725a65726f5f6f6e65}\textbf{Char zero one.} Let \(M\) be a type. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Add\,Monoid\,With\,One}\left(M\right)\). Let \(\mathit{inst}_{1}\) be a proof of \(\operatorname{Char\,Zero}\left(M\right)\). Then \(\operatorname{Ne\,Zero}\left(1\right)\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:50726f642e657869737473}\paperlink{premise:50726f642e657869737473}{P120.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:50726f642e657869737473}\textbf{Exists.} Let \(\alpha\) and \(\beta\) be types. Let \(p\) be a function of type \(\alpha \times \beta \to \mathsf{Prop}\). Then \((\exists x\, :\, \alpha \times \beta,\; p(x)) \iff (\exists a\, :\, \alpha,\; \exists b\, :\, \beta,\; p((a,\allowbreak{} b)))\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:50726f642e6d6b2e696e6a4571}\paperlink{premise:50726f642e6d6b2e696e6a4571}{P121.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:50726f642e6d6b2e696e6a4571}\textbf{Injectivity equals.} Let \(\alpha\) and \(\beta\) be types. Let \(\mathit{fst}\) be an element of \(\alpha\). Let \(\mathit{snd}\) be an element of \(\beta\). Let \(\mathit{fst}_{1}\) be an element of \(\alpha\). Let \(\mathit{snd}_{1}\) be an element of \(\beta\). Then \(((\mathit{fst},\allowbreak{} \mathit{snd}) = (\mathit{fst}_{1},\allowbreak{} \mathit{snd}_{1})) = (\mathit{fst} = \mathit{fst}_{1} \land \mathit{snd} = \mathit{snd}_{1})\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:5261742e696e73744164644c6566744d6f6e6f}\paperlink{premise:5261742e696e73744164644c6566744d6f6e6f}{P122.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:5261742e696e73744164644c6566744d6f6e6f}\textbf{Inst sum left mono.} \(\operatorname{Add\,Left\,Mono}\left(\mathbb{Q}\right)\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:5261742e696e7374436861725a65726f}\paperlink{premise:5261742e696e7374436861725a65726f}{P123.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:5261742e696e7374436861725a65726f}\textbf{Inst char zero.} \(\operatorname{Char\,Zero}\left(\mathbb{Q}\right)\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:5261742e696e737449735374726963744f72646572656452696e67}\paperlink{premise:5261742e696e737449735374726963744f72646572656452696e67}{P124.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:5261742e696e737449735374726963744f72646572656452696e67}\textbf{Inst is strict ordered ring.} \(\mathbb{Q}\text{ is a strictly ordered semiring}\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:5261742e6e6f6e7472697669616c}\paperlink{premise:5261742e6e6f6e7472697669616c}{P125.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:5261742e6e6f6e7472697669616c}\textbf{Nontrivial.} \(\operatorname{Nontrivial}\left(\mathbb{Q}\right)\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:5365742e657874}\paperlink{premise:5365742e657874}{P126.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:5365742e657874}\textbf{Ext.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Let \(a\) and \(b\) be subsets of \(\alpha\). Assume \(\forall x\, :\, \alpha,\; x \in a \iff x \in b\). Then \(a = b\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:537562747970652e657869737473}\paperlink{premise:537562747970652e657869737473}{P127.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:537562747970652e657869737473}\textbf{Exists.} Let \(\alpha\) be a sort. Let \(p\) be a function of type \(\alpha \to \mathsf{Prop}\). Let \(q\) be a function of type \(\{a\in \alpha \mid p\left(a\right)\} \to \mathsf{Prop}\). Then \((\exists x\, :\, \{a\in \alpha \mid p(a)\},\; q(x)) \iff (\exists a\, :\, \alpha,\; \exists b\text{ proving }p(a),\; q((a\, :\, \{a_{1}\in \alpha \mid p(a_{1})\})))\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:5472616e732e73696d706c65}\paperlink{premise:5472616e732e73696d706c65}{P128.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:5472616e732e73696d706c65}\textbf{Simple.} Let \(\alpha\) be a sort. Let \(a\), \(b\), and \(c\) be elements of \(\alpha\). Let \(r\) be a relation from \(\alpha\) to \(\alpha\). Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Trans}\left(r, r, r\right)\). Assume \(r\left(a, b\right)\). Assume \(r\left(b, c\right)\). Then \(r\left(a, c\right)\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:547265652e6e6f436f6e667573696f6e}\paperlink{premise:547265652e6e6f436f6e667573696f6e}{P129.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:547265652e6e6f436f6e667573696f6e}\textbf{No confusion.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Let \(P\) be a proposition. Let \(\mathit{v1}\) be an element of \(\operatorname{Tree}\left(\alpha\right)\). Let \(\mathit{v2}\) be an element of \(\operatorname{Tree}\left(\alpha\right)\). Assume \(\mathit{v1} = \mathit{v2}\). Then \(\operatorname{Tree\mathord{:}no\,Confusion\,Type}\left(P, \mathit{v1}, \mathit{v2}\right)\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:547265652e6e6f64652e696e6a4571}\paperlink{premise:547265652e6e6f64652e696e6a4571}{P130.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:547265652e6e6f64652e696e6a4571}\textbf{Injectivity equals.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Let \(a\) be an element of \(\alpha\). Let \(a_{1}\) be an element of \(\operatorname{Tree}\left(\alpha\right)\). Let \(a_{2}\) be an element of \(\operatorname{Tree}\left(\alpha\right)\). Let \(a_{3}\) be an element of \(\alpha\). Let \(a_{4}\) be an element of \(\operatorname{Tree}\left(\alpha\right)\). Let \(a_{5}\) be an element of \(\operatorname{Tree}\left(\alpha\right)\). Then \((\operatorname{node}(a,\allowbreak{} a_{1},\allowbreak{} a_{2}) = \operatorname{node}(a_{3},\allowbreak{} a_{4},\allowbreak{} a_{5})) = (a = a_{3} \land (a_{1} = a_{4} \land a_{2} = a_{5}))\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:547265652e756e69745265634f6e}\paperlink{premise:547265652e756e69745265634f6e}{P131.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:547265652e756e69745265634f6e}\textbf{Unit rec on.} Let \(\mathit{motive}\) be a function of type \(\operatorname{Tree}\left(\operatorname{Unit}\right) \to \mathsf{Prop}\). Let \(t\) be an element of \(\operatorname{Tree}\left(\operatorname{Unit}\right)\). Assume \(\mathit{motive}\left(\operatorname{Tree\mathord{:}nil}\right)\). Assume \(\forall x\, :\, \operatorname{Tree}(\operatorname{Unit}),\; \forall y\, :\, \operatorname{Tree}(\operatorname{Unit}),\; \mathit{motive}(x) \implies \mathit{motive}(y) \implies \mathit{motive}(\operatorname{node}(\operatorname{unit},\allowbreak{} x,\allowbreak{} y))\). Then \(\mathit{motive}\left(t\right)\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:6164645f636f6d6d}\paperlink{premise:6164645f636f6d6d}{P132.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:6164645f636f6d6d}\textbf{Addition commutativity.} Let \(G\) be a type. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Add\,Comm\,Magma}\left(G\right)\). Let \(a\) and \(b\) be elements of \(G\). Then \(a + b = b + a\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:6164645f706f73}\paperlink{premise:6164645f706f73}{P133.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:6164645f706f73}\textbf{Alias.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Add\,Zero\,Class}\left(\alpha\right)\). Equip \(\alpha\) with a preorder. Let \(\mathit{inst}_{2}\) be a proof of \(\operatorname{Add\,Left\,Strict\,Mono}\left(\alpha\right)\). Let \(a\) and \(b\) be elements of \(\alpha\). Assume \(0 < a\). Assume \(0 < b\). Then \(0 < a + b\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:6164645f7a65726f}\paperlink{premise:6164645f7a65726f}{P134.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:6164645f7a65726f}\textbf{Sum zero.} Let \(M\) be a type. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Add\,Zero\,Class}\left(M\right)\). Let \(a\) be an element of \(M\). Then \(a + 0 = a\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:616e645f66616c7365}\paperlink{premise:616e645f66616c7365}{P135.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:616e645f66616c7365}\textbf{And false.} Let \(p\) be a proposition. Then \(\left(p \land \mathrm{False}\right) = \mathrm{False}\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:616e645f696d70}\paperlink{premise:616e645f696d70}{P136.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:616e645f696d70}\textbf{And imp.} Let \(a\), \(b\), and \(c\) be propositions. Then \(a \land b \implies c \iff a \implies b \implies c\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:6469765f646976}\paperlink{premise:6469765f646976}{P137.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:6469765f646976}\textbf{Div div.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Division\,Comm\,Monoid}\left(\alpha\right)\). Let \(a\), \(b\), and \(c\) be elements of \(\alpha\). Then \(a / b / c = a / \left(b \cdot c\right)\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:6469765f65715f696666}\paperlink{premise:6469765f65715f696666}{P138.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:6469765f65715f696666}\textbf{Div equals if and only if.} Let \(G_{0}\) be a type. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Group\,With\,Zero}\left(G_{0}\right)\). Let \(a\), \(b\), and \(c\) be elements of \(G_{0}\). Assume \(b \ne 0\). Then \(a / b = c \iff a = c \cdot b\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:6469765f6d756c5f65715f6d756c5f646976}\paperlink{premise:6469765f6d756c5f65715f6d756c5f646976}{P139.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:6469765f6d756c5f65715f6d756c5f646976}\textbf{Div product equals product div.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Division\,Comm\,Monoid}\left(\alpha\right)\). Let \(a\), \(b\), and \(c\) be elements of \(\alpha\). Then \(a / b \cdot c = a \cdot c / b\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:6469765f73656c66}\paperlink{premise:6469765f73656c66}{P140.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:6469765f73656c66}\textbf{Div self.} Let \(G_{0}\) be a type. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Group\,With\,Zero}\left(G_{0}\right)\). Let \(a\) be an element of \(G_{0}\). Assume \(a \ne 0\). Then \(a / a = 1\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:6469765f73756227}\paperlink{premise:6469765f73756227}{P141.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:6469765f73756227}\textbf{Div sub'.} Let \(K\) be a type. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Field}\left(K\right)\). Let \(a\), \(b\), and \(c\) be elements of \(K\). Assume \(c \ne 0\). Then \(a / c - b = \left(a - c \cdot b\right) / c\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:65715f6469765f696666}\paperlink{premise:65715f6469765f696666}{P142.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:65715f6469765f696666}\textbf{Equals div if and only if.} Let \(G_{0}\) be a type. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Group\,With\,Zero}\left(G_{0}\right)\). Let \(a\), \(b\), and \(c\) be elements of \(G_{0}\). Assume \(b \ne 0\). Then \(c = a / b \iff c \cdot b = a\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:65715f66616c7365}\paperlink{premise:65715f66616c7365}{P143.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:65715f66616c7365}\textbf{Equals false.} Let \(p\) be a proposition. Assume \(\neg p\). Then \(p = \mathrm{False}\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:65715f66616c736527}\paperlink{premise:65715f66616c736527}{P144.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:65715f66616c736527}\textbf{Equals false'.} Let \(p\) be a proposition. Assume \(p \implies \mathrm{False}\). Then \(p = \mathrm{False}\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:65715f73656c66}\paperlink{premise:65715f73656c66}{P145.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:65715f73656c66}\textbf{Equals self.} Let \(\alpha\) be a sort. Let \(a\) be an element of \(\alpha\). Then \(\left(a = a\right) = \mathrm{True}\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:65715f74727565}\paperlink{premise:65715f74727565}{P146.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:65715f74727565}\textbf{Equals true.} Let \(p\) be a proposition. Assume \(p\). Then \(p = \mathrm{True}\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:6578697374735f636f6e7374}\paperlink{premise:6578697374735f636f6e7374}{P147.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:6578697374735f636f6e7374}\textbf{Exists constant.} Let \(b\) be a proposition. Let \(\alpha\) be a sort. Assume \(\alpha\text{ is nonempty}\). Then \(\left(\exists x\, :\, \alpha,\; b\right) \iff b\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:6578697374735f65715f7269676874}\paperlink{premise:6578697374735f65715f7269676874}{P148.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:6578697374735f65715f7269676874}\textbf{Exists equals right.} Let \(\alpha\) be a sort. Let \(p\) be a function of type \(\alpha \to \mathsf{Prop}\). Let \(a^{'}\) be an element of \(\alpha\). Then \(\left(\exists a\, :\, \alpha,\; p\left(a\right) \land a = a^{'}\right) \iff p\left(a^{'}\right)\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:6578697374735f65715f726967687427}\paperlink{premise:6578697374735f65715f726967687427}{P149.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:6578697374735f65715f726967687427}\textbf{Exists equals right'.} Let \(\alpha\) be a sort. Let \(p\) be a function of type \(\alpha \to \mathsf{Prop}\). Let \(a^{'}\) be an element of \(\alpha\). Then \(\left(\exists a\, :\, \alpha,\; p\left(a\right) \land a^{'} = a\right) \iff p\left(a^{'}\right)\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:6578697374735f65715f72696768745f7269676874}\paperlink{premise:6578697374735f65715f72696768745f7269676874}{P150.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:6578697374735f65715f72696768745f7269676874}\textbf{Exists equals right right.} Let \(\alpha\) be a sort. Then \((\exists a\, :\, \alpha,\; p(a) \land (q(a) \land a = a^{'})) \iff p(a^{'}) \land q(a^{'})\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:6578697374735f65715f72696768745f726967687427}\paperlink{premise:6578697374735f65715f72696768745f726967687427}{P151.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:6578697374735f65715f72696768745f726967687427}\textbf{Exists equals right right'.} Let \(\alpha\) be a sort. Let \(p\) and \(q\) be functions of type \(\alpha \to \mathsf{Prop}\). Let \(a^{'}\) be an element of \(\alpha\). Then \((\exists a\, :\, \alpha,\; p(a) \land (q(a) \land a^{'} = a)) \iff p(a^{'}) \land q(a^{'})\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:6578697374735f70726f70}\paperlink{premise:6578697374735f70726f70}{P152.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:6578697374735f70726f70}\textbf{Exists prop.} Let \(b\) and \(a\) be propositions. Then \(a \land b \iff a \land b\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:6578697374735f70726f705f636f6e6772}\paperlink{premise:6578697374735f70726f705f636f6e6772}{P153.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:6578697374735f70726f705f636f6e6772}\textbf{Exists prop congruence.} Let \(p\) and \(p^{'}\) be propositions. Let \(\mathit{hp}\) be a proof of \(p \iff p^{'}\). Then \((\exists x\, :\, p,\; q(x)) \iff (\exists h\text{ proving }p^{'},\; q^{'}(\operatorname{Iff\mathord{:}mpr}(\mathit{hp},\allowbreak{} h)))\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:666f72616c6c5f636f6e6772}\paperlink{premise:666f72616c6c5f636f6e6772}{P154.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:666f72616c6c5f636f6e6772}\textbf{Forall congruence.} Let \(\alpha\) be a sort. Then \((\forall a\, :\, \alpha,\; p(a)) = (\forall a\, :\, \alpha,\; q(a))\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:666f72616c6c5f636f6e7374}\paperlink{premise:666f72616c6c5f636f6e7374}{P155.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:666f72616c6c5f636f6e7374}\textbf{Forall constant.} Let \(b\) be a proposition. Let \(g\) be a function of type \(\Pi_{x\, :\, \alpha}\, \beta\left(x\right)\). Assume \(\forall x\, :\, \alpha,\; f\left(x\right) = g\left(x\right)\). Then \(f = g\).
