Equip \(\alpha\) with a preorder. Let \(a\) and \(b\) be elements of \(\alpha\). Assume \(b < a\). Then \(a \ne b\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:6e65675f6164645f726576}\paperlink{premise:6e65675f6164645f726576}{P172.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:6e65675f6164645f726576}\textbf{Neg sum rev.} Let \(G\) be a type. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Subtraction\,Monoid}\left(G\right)\). Let \(a\) and \(b\) be elements of \(G\). Then \(-\left(a + b\right) = -b + -a\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:6e6f745f616e64}\paperlink{premise:6e6f745f616e64}{P173.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:6e6f745f616e64}\textbf{Not and.} Let \(a\) and \(b\) be propositions. Then \(\neg \left(a \land b\right) \iff a \implies \neg b\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:6e6f745f657869737473}\paperlink{premise:6e6f745f657869737473}{P174.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:6e6f745f657869737473}\textbf{Not exists.} Let \(\alpha\) be a sort. Let \(p\) be a function of type \(\alpha \to \mathsf{Prop}\). Then \(\neg (\exists x\, :\, \alpha,\; p(x)) \iff (\forall x\, :\, \alpha,\; \neg p(x))\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:6e6f745f66616c73655f65715f74727565}\paperlink{premise:6e6f745f66616c73655f65715f74727565}{P175.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:6e6f745f66616c73655f65715f74727565}\textbf{Not false equals true.} \(\left(\neg \mathrm{False}\right) = \mathrm{True}\).

Assume \(p\) is decidable. Assume \(\operatorname{decide}\left(p\right) = \mathrm{true}\). Then \(p\).

Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Mul\,One\,Class}\left(M\right)\). Let \(a\) be an element of \(M\). Then \(1 \cdot a = a\).

Let \(\mathit{inst}_{2}\) be a proof of \(\operatorname{Ne\,Zero}\left(1\right)\). Then \(1 \ne 0\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:72696768745f65715f616464}\paperlink{premise:72696768745f65715f616464}{P179.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:72696768745f65715f616464}\textbf{Right equals sum.} Let \(M\) be a type. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Add\,Right\,Cancel\,Monoid}\left(M\right)\). Let \(a\) and \(b\) be elements of \(M\). Then \(b = a + b \iff a = 0\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:7375625f64697627}\paperlink{premise:7375625f64697627}{P180.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:7375625f64697627}\textbf{Difference div'.} Let \(K\) be a type. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Field}\left(K\right)\). Let \(a\), \(b\), and \(c\) be elements of \(K\). Assume \(c \ne 0\). Then \(b - a / c = \left(b \cdot c - a\right) / c\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:747275655f616e64}\paperlink{premise:747275655f616e64}{P181.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:747275655f616e64}\textbf{True and.} Let \(p\) be a proposition. Then \(\left(\mathrm{True} \land p\right) = p\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:747375625f6c655f73656c66}\paperlink{premise:747375625f6c655f73656c66}{P182.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:747375625f6c655f73656c66}\textbf{Tsub at most self.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Equip \(\alpha\) with a commutative additive monoid structure and a partial order. Let \(\mathit{inst}_{2}\) be a proof of \(\operatorname{Canonically\,Ordered\,Add}\left(\alpha\right)\). Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}_{3}\) of type \(\operatorname{Sub}\left(\alpha\right)\). Let \(\mathit{inst}_{4}\) be a proof of \(\operatorname{Ordered\,Sub}\left(\alpha\right)\). Let \(a\) and \(b\) be elements of \(\alpha\). Then \(a - b \le a\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:747375625f73656c66}\paperlink{premise:747375625f73656c66}{P183.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:747375625f73656c66}\textbf{Tsub self.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Equip \(\alpha\) with a commutative additive monoid structure and a partial order. Let \(\mathit{inst}_{2}\) be a proof of \(\operatorname{Canonically\,Ordered\,Add}\left(\alpha\right)\). Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}_{3}\) of type \(\operatorname{Sub}\left(\alpha\right)\). Let \(\mathit{inst}_{4}\) be a proof of \(\operatorname{Ordered\,Sub}\left(\alpha\right)\). Let \(a\) be an element of \(\alpha\). Then \(a - a = 0\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:7a65726f5f616464}\paperlink{premise:7a65726f5f616464}{P184.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:7a65726f5f616464}\textbf{Zero sum.} Let \(M\) be a type. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Add\,Zero\,Class}\left(M\right)\). Let \(a\) be an element of \(M\). Then \(0 + a = a\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:7a65726f5f737562}\paperlink{premise:7a65726f5f737562}{P185.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:7a65726f5f737562}\textbf{Zero difference.} Let \(G\) be a type. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Sub\,Neg\,Monoid}\left(G\right)\). Let \(a\) be an element of \(G\). Then \(0 - a = -a\).

\section{Results}
\begin{lemma}\label{theorem:1}
\(\operatorname{catalan}\left(0\right) = 1\).
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Rewriting with \paperlink{defeq:1}{defining equation~D\ref{defeq:1}} gives \(\operatorname{catalan}\left(0\right) = 1\).
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}\label{theorem:2}
Let \(n\) be a natural number. Then
\begin{center}
\(\displaystyle \operatorname{catalan}(n + 1) = \sum_{i\in \operatorname{Fin}(n + 1)}(\operatorname{catalan}(\operatorname{val}(i)) \cdot \operatorname{catalan}(n - \operatorname{val}(i))).\)
\end{center}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Rewriting with \paperlink{defeq:2}{defining equation~D\ref{defeq:2}} gives \(\operatorname{catalan}(n + 1) = \sum_{i\in \operatorname{Fin}(n + 1)}(\operatorname{catalan}(\operatorname{val}(i)) \cdot \operatorname{catalan}(n - \operatorname{val}(i)))\).
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}\label{theorem:3}
Let \(n\) be a natural number. Then
\begin{center}
\(\displaystyle \operatorname{catalan}(n + 1) = \sum_{\mathit{ij}\in \operatorname{antidiagonal}(n)}(\operatorname{catalan}(\pi_1(\mathit{ij})) \cdot \operatorname{catalan}(\pi_2(\mathit{ij}))).\)
\end{center}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
By \paperlink{theorem:2}{Lemma~\ref{theorem:2}}, we obtain \(\operatorname{catalan}(n + 1) = \sum_{i\in \operatorname{Fin}(n + 1)}(\operatorname{catalan}(\operatorname{val}(i)) \cdot \operatorname{catalan}(n - \operatorname{val}(i)))\).
Applying the same function to equal arguments, we obtain \((\operatorname{catalan}(n + 1) = \sum_{\mathit{ij}\in \operatorname{antidiagonal}(n)}(\operatorname{catalan}(\pi_1(\mathit{ij})) \cdot \operatorname{catalan}(\pi_2(\mathit{ij})))) = (\sum_{i\in \operatorname{Fin}(n + 1)}(\operatorname{catalan}(\operatorname{val}(i)) \cdot \operatorname{catalan}(n - \operatorname{val}(i))) = \sum_{\mathit{ij}\in \operatorname{antidiagonal}(n)}(\operatorname{catalan}(\pi_1(\mathit{ij})) \cdot \operatorname{catalan}(\pi_2(\mathit{ij}))))\).
By the preceding argument, we obtain \((\operatorname{catalan}(n + 1) = \sum_{\mathit{ij}\in \operatorname{antidiagonal}(n)}(\operatorname{catalan}(\pi_1(\mathit{ij})) \cdot \operatorname{catalan}(\pi_2(\mathit{ij})))) = (\sum_{i\in \operatorname{Fin}(n + 1)}(\operatorname{catalan}(\operatorname{val}(i)) \cdot \operatorname{catalan}(n - \operatorname{val}(i))) = \sum_{\mathit{ij}\in \operatorname{antidiagonal}(n)}(\operatorname{catalan}(\pi_1(\mathit{ij})) \cdot \operatorname{catalan}(\pi_2(\mathit{ij}))))\).
By \paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e4e61742e73756d5f616e7469646961676f6e616c5f65715f73756d5f72616e67655f73756363}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:46696e7365742e4e61742e73756d5f616e7469646961676f6e616c5f65715f73756d5f72616e67655f73756363}} with \(M = \mathbb{N}\) and \(f = x \mapsto y \mapsto \operatorname{catalan}\left(x\right) \cdot \operatorname{catalan}\left(y\right)\), we obtain \(\sum_{\mathit{ij}\in \operatorname{antidiagonal}(n)}(\operatorname{catalan}(\pi_1(\mathit{ij})) \cdot \operatorname{catalan}(\pi_2(\mathit{ij}))) = \sum_{0\le k<n + 1}(\operatorname{catalan}(k) \cdot \operatorname{catalan}(n - k))\).
Applying the same function to equal arguments, we obtain \((\sum_{i\in \operatorname{Fin}(n + 1)}(\operatorname{catalan}(\operatorname{val}(i)) \cdot \operatorname{catalan}(n - \operatorname{val}(i))) = \sum_{\mathit{ij}\in \operatorname{antidiagonal}(n)}(\operatorname{catalan}(\pi_1(\mathit{ij})) \cdot \operatorname{catalan}(\pi_2(\mathit{ij})))) = (\sum_{i\in \operatorname{Fin}(n + 1)}(\operatorname{catalan}(\operatorname{val}(i)) \cdot \operatorname{catalan}(n - \operatorname{val}(i))) = \sum_{0\le k<n + 1}(\operatorname{catalan}(k) \cdot \operatorname{catalan}(n - k)))\).
By the preceding argument, we obtain \((\sum_{i\in \operatorname{Fin}(n + 1)}(\operatorname{catalan}(\operatorname{val}(i)) \cdot \operatorname{catalan}(n - \operatorname{val}(i))) = \sum_{\mathit{ij}\in \operatorname{antidiagonal}(n)}(\operatorname{catalan}(\pi_1(\mathit{ij})) \cdot \operatorname{catalan}(\pi_2(\mathit{ij})))) = (\sum_{i\in \operatorname{Fin}(n + 1)}(\operatorname{catalan}(\operatorname{val}(i)) \cdot \operatorname{catalan}(n - \operatorname{val}(i))) = \sum_{0\le k<n + 1}(\operatorname{catalan}(k) \cdot \operatorname{catalan}(n - k)))\).
By \paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e73756d5f72616e6765}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:46696e7365742e73756d5f72616e6765}} with \(M = \mathbb{N}\) and \(f = k \mapsto \operatorname{catalan}\left(k\right) \cdot \operatorname{catalan}\left(n - k\right)\), we obtain \(\sum_{0\le i<n + 1}(\operatorname{catalan}(i) \cdot \operatorname{catalan}(n - i)) = \sum_{i\in \operatorname{Fin}(n + 1)}(\operatorname{catalan}(\operatorname{val}(i)) \cdot \operatorname{catalan}(n - \operatorname{val}(i)))\).
Applying the same function to equal arguments, we obtain \((\sum_{i\in \operatorname{Fin}(n + 1)}(\operatorname{catalan}(\operatorname{val}(i)) \cdot \operatorname{catalan}(n - \operatorname{val}(i))) = \sum_{0\le i<n + 1}(\operatorname{catalan}(i) \cdot \operatorname{catalan}(n - i))) = (\sum_{i\in \operatorname{Fin}(n + 1)}(\operatorname{catalan}(\operatorname{val}(i)) \cdot \operatorname{catalan}(n - \operatorname{val}(i))) = \sum_{i\in \operatorname{Fin}(n + 1)}(\operatorname{catalan}(\operatorname{val}(i)) \cdot \operatorname{catalan}(n - \operatorname{val}(i))))\).
By the preceding argument, we obtain \((\sum_{i\in \operatorname{Fin}(n + 1)}(\operatorname{catalan}(\operatorname{val}(i)) \cdot \operatorname{catalan}(n - \operatorname{val}(i))) = \sum_{0\le k<n + 1}(\operatorname{catalan}(k) \cdot \operatorname{catalan}(n - k))) = (\sum_{i\in \operatorname{Fin}(n + 1)}(\operatorname{catalan}(\operatorname{val}(i)) \cdot \operatorname{catalan}(n - \operatorname{val}(i))) = \sum_{i\in \operatorname{Fin}(n + 1)}(\operatorname{catalan}(\operatorname{val}(i)) \cdot \operatorname{catalan}(n - \operatorname{val}(i))))\).
By definition, we obtain \(\sum_{i\in \operatorname{Fin}(n + 1)}(\operatorname{catalan}(\operatorname{val}(i)) \cdot \operatorname{catalan}(n - \operatorname{val}(i))) = \sum_{i\in \operatorname{Fin}(n + 1)}(\operatorname{catalan}(\operatorname{val}(i)) \cdot \operatorname{catalan}(n - \operatorname{val}(i)))\).
Substituting along the equality, we obtain \(\sum_{i\in \operatorname{Fin}(n + 1)}(\operatorname{catalan}(\operatorname{val}(i)) \cdot \operatorname{catalan}(n - \operatorname{val}(i))) = \sum_{0\le k<n + 1}(\operatorname{catalan}(k) \cdot \operatorname{catalan}(n - k))\).
Substituting along the equality, we obtain \(\sum_{i\in \operatorname{Fin}(n + 1)}(\operatorname{catalan}(\operatorname{val}(i)) \cdot \operatorname{catalan}(n - \operatorname{val}(i))) = \sum_{\mathit{ij}\in \operatorname{antidiagonal}(n)}(\operatorname{catalan}(\pi_1(\mathit{ij})) \cdot \operatorname{catalan}(\pi_2(\mathit{ij})))\).
Substituting along the equality, we obtain \(\operatorname{catalan}(n + 1) = \sum_{\mathit{ij}\in \operatorname{antidiagonal}(n)}(\operatorname{catalan}(\pi_1(\mathit{ij})) \cdot \operatorname{catalan}(\pi_2(\mathit{ij})))\).
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}\label{theorem:4}
\(\operatorname{catalan}\left(1\right) = 1\).
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The substitution uses the following argument.
\begin{proofstep}
Fix \(x\) in \(\operatorname{Fin}(0 + 1)\).
Assume \(x \in \{0\}\).
By \paperlink{premise:46696e2e76616c5f65715f7a65726f}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:46696e2e76616c5f65715f7a65726f}} with \(x\) in place of its parameter \(a\), we obtain \(\operatorname{val}(x) = 0\).
Applying the same function to equal arguments, we obtain \(\operatorname{catalan}\left(\operatorname{val}(x)\right) = \operatorname{catalan}\left(0\right)\).
By \paperlink{theorem:1}{Lemma~\ref{theorem:1}}, we obtain \(\operatorname{catalan}\left(0\right) = 1\).
Chaining the equalities, we obtain \(\operatorname{catalan}\left(\operatorname{val}(x)\right) = 1\).
Applying the same function to equal arguments, we obtain \((y \mapsto \operatorname{catalan}(\operatorname{val}(x)) \cdot y) = (y \mapsto 1 \cdot y)\).
By \paperlink{premise:46696e2e76616c5f65715f7a65726f}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:46696e2e76616c5f65715f7a65726f}} with \(x\) in place of its parameter \(a\), we obtain \(\operatorname{val}(x) = 0\).
Applying the same function to equal arguments, we obtain \(0 - \operatorname{val}(x) = 0 - 0\).
By \paperlink{premise:4e61742e696e737443616e6f6e6963616c6c794f726465726564416464}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4e61742e696e737443616e6f6e6963616c6c794f726465726564416464}}, we obtain \(\operatorname{Canonically\,Ordered\,Add}\left(\mathbb{N}\right)\).
By \paperlink{premise:4e61742e696e73744f726465726564537562}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4e61742e696e73744f726465726564537562}}, we obtain \(\operatorname{Ordered\,Sub}\left(\mathbb{N}\right)\).
By \paperlink{premise:747375625f73656c66}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:747375625f73656c66}} with \(\alpha = \mathbb{N}\) and \(0\) in place of its parameter \(a\), we obtain \(0 - 0 = 0\).
Chaining the equalities, we obtain \(0 - \operatorname{val}(x) = 0\).
Applying the same function to equal arguments, we obtain \(\operatorname{catalan}\left(0 - \operatorname{val}(x)\right) = \operatorname{catalan}\left(0\right)\).
By \paperlink{theorem:1}{Lemma~\ref{theorem:1}}, we obtain \(\operatorname{catalan}\left(0\right) = 1\).
Chaining the equalities, we obtain \(\operatorname{catalan}\left(0 - \operatorname{val}(x)\right) = 1\).
By congruence, we obtain \(\operatorname{catalan}(\operatorname{val}(x)) \cdot \operatorname{catalan}(0 - \operatorname{val}(x)) = 1 \cdot 1\).
By \paperlink{premise:6d756c5f6f6e65}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:6d756c5f6f6e65}} with \(M = \mathbb{N}\) and \(1\) in place of its parameter \(a\), we obtain \(1 \cdot 1 = 1\).
Chaining the equalities, we obtain \(\operatorname{catalan}(\operatorname{val}(x)) \cdot \operatorname{catalan}(0 - \operatorname{val}(x)) = 1\).
\end{proofstep}
Rewriting with \paperlink{theorem:2}{Lemma~\ref{theorem:2}}, \paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e73756d5f636f6e6772}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:46696e7365742e73756d5f636f6e6772}}, \paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e756e69765f756e69717565}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:46696e7365742e756e69765f756e69717565}}, \paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e73756d5f636f6e7374}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:46696e7365742e73756d5f636f6e7374}}, \paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e636172645f73696e676c65746f6e}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:46696e7365742e636172645f73696e676c65746f6e}}, \paperlink{premise:6d756c5f6f6e65}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:6d756c5f6f6e65}} and the identity established above gives \(\operatorname{catalan}\left(1\right) = 1\).
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[{The central binomial formula for Catalan numbers}]\label{theorem:5}
Let \(n\) be a natural number. Then \(\operatorname{catalan}\left(n\right) = \binom{2 \cdot n}{n} / \left(n + 1\right)\).
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Proceed by strong induction on \(n\).
\emph{Base case.}
\begin{proofstep}
Rewriting with \paperlink{theorem:1}{Lemma~\ref{theorem:1}}, \paperlink{premise:4e61742e636173745f6f6e65}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4e61742e636173745f6f6e65}}, \paperlink{premise:4e61742e63656e7472616c42696e6f6d5f7a65726f}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4e61742e63656e7472616c42696e6f6d5f7a65726f}}, \paperlink{premise:4e61742e636173745f7a65726f}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4e61742e636173745f7a65726f}}, \paperlink{premise:7a65726f5f616464}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:7a65726f5f616464}}, \paperlink{premise:6469765f73656c66}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:6469765f73656c66}}, \paperlink{premise:65715f66616c7365}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:65715f66616c7365}}, \paperlink{premise:6f6e655f6e655f7a65726f}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:6f6e655f6e655f7a65726f}}, \paperlink{premise:4e655a65726f2e636861725a65726f5f6f6e65}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4e655a65726f2e636861725a65726f5f6f6e65}}, \paperlink{premise:5261742e696e7374436861725a65726f}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:5261742e696e7374436861725a65726f}} and \paperlink{premise:6e6f745f66616c73655f65715f74727565}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:6e6f745f66616c73655f65715f74727565}} gives \(\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(\operatorname{catalan}(0)) = \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(\binom{2 \cdot 0}{0}) / (\operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(0) + 1)\).
\end{proofstep}
\emph{Successor step.}
\begin{proofstep}
Fix \(d\) in \(\mathbb{N}\).
Assume as induction hypothesis that \(\forall m\, :\, \mathbb{N},\; m \le d \implies \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(\operatorname{catalan}(m)) = \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(\binom{2 \cdot m}{m}) / (\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(m) + 1)\).
The substitution uses the following argument.
\begin{proofstep}
Fix \(x\) in \(\operatorname{Fin}(d + 1)\).
Assume \(x \in \operatorname{Fin}(d + 1)\).
By \paperlink{premise:4e61742e636173745f6d756c}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4e61742e636173745f6d756c}} with \(\alpha = \mathbb{Q}\) and \(m = \operatorname{catalan}\left(\operatorname{val}(x)\right)\) and \(\operatorname{catalan}\left(d - \operatorname{val}(x)\right)\) in place of its parameter \(n\), we obtain \(\operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(\operatorname{catalan}(\operatorname{val}(x)) \cdot \operatorname{catalan}(d - \operatorname{val}(x))) = \operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(\operatorname{catalan}(\operatorname{val}(x))) \cdot \operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(\operatorname{catalan}(d - \operatorname{val}(x)))\).
\end{proofstep}
For the required implication, argue as follows.
\begin{proofstep}
Fix \(f\) in \(\operatorname{Fin}(d + 1) \to \mathbb{Q}\).
Fix \(f_{1}\) in \(\operatorname{Fin}(d + 1) \to \mathbb{Q}\).
Assume \(f = f_{1}\).
By definition, we obtain \(\sum_{i\in \operatorname{Fin}(d + 1)}(f(i)) = \sum_{i\in \operatorname{Fin}(d + 1)}(f(i))\).
Use the hypothesis \(f = f_{1}\).
Substituting equals, we obtain \(\sum_{i\in \operatorname{Fin}(d + 1)}(f(i)) = \sum_{i\in \operatorname{Fin}(d + 1)}(f_{1}(i))\).
\end{proofstep}
By definition, we obtain \(\operatorname{Fin}(d + 1) = \operatorname{Fin}(d + 1)\).
For the required implication, argue as follows.
\begin{proofstep}
Fix \(x\) in \(\operatorname{Fin}(d + 1)\).
Consider the following case.
\begin{proofstep}
Assume \(\neg \left(\operatorname{val}(x) \le d\right)\).
By definition, we obtain \(\operatorname{decide}(\operatorname{is\,Impossible}(\operatorname{Lean\mathord{:}Omega\mathord{:}Constraint\mathord{:}mk}(\operatorname{some}(1),\allowbreak{} \operatorname{some}(0))) = \mathrm{true}) = \mathrm{true}\).
By \paperlink{premise:6f665f6465636964655f65715f74727565}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:6f665f6465636964655f65715f74727565}}, we obtain \(\operatorname{is\,Impossible}(\operatorname{Lean\mathord{:}Omega\mathord{:}Constraint\mathord{:}mk}(\operatorname{some}(1),\allowbreak{} \operatorname{some}(0))) = \mathrm{true}\).
By \paperlink{premise:46696e2e69734c74}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:46696e2e69734c74}} with \(\mathit{self} = x\), we obtain \(\operatorname{val}(x) < d + 1\).
By \paperlink{premise:4c65616e2e4f6d6567612e496e742e6f664e61745f6c745f6f665f6c74}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4c65616e2e4f6d6567612e496e742e6f664e61745f6c745f6f665f6c74}}, we obtain \(\operatorname{val}(x) < d + 1\).
By \paperlink{premise:496e742e6164645f6f6e655f6c655f6f665f6c74}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:496e742e6164645f6f6e655f6c655f6f665f6c74}}, we obtain \(\operatorname{val}(x) + 1 \le d + 1\).
By \paperlink{premise:496e742e7375625f6e6f6e6e65675f6f665f6c65}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:496e742e7375625f6e6f6e6e65675f6f665f6c65}}, we obtain \(0 \le d + 1 - \left(\operatorname{val}(x) + 1\right)\).
By \paperlink{premise:496e742e6f664e61745f73756363}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:496e742e6f664e61745f73756363}} with \(d\) in place of its parameter \(n\), we obtain \(d + 1 = d + 1\).
Using \paperlink{premise:4c65616e2e4f6d6567612e4c696e656172436f6d626f2e636f6f7264696e6174655f6576616c5f31}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4c65616e2e4f6d6567612e4c696e656172436f6d626f2e636f6f7264696e6174655f6576616c5f31}} in reverse gives \(d = \operatorname{eval}(\operatorname{coordinate}(1),\allowbreak{} \operatorname{of\,List}(\langle \operatorname{val}(x)\rangle \mathbin{\Vert} \langle d\rangle))\).
By definition, we obtain \(1 = \operatorname{eval}(\operatorname{Lean\mathord{:}Omega\mathord{:}Linear\,Combo\mathord{:}mk}(1,\allowbreak{} \langle\,\rangle),\allowbreak{} \operatorname{of\,List}(\langle \operatorname{val}(x)\rangle \mathbin{\Vert} \langle d\rangle))\).
By \paperlink{premise:4c65616e2e4f6d6567612e496e742e6164645f636f6e6772}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4c65616e2e4f6d6567612e496e742e6164645f636f6e6772}}, we obtain \(d + 1 = \operatorname{eval}(\operatorname{coordinate}(1),\allowbreak{} \operatorname{of\,List}(\langle \operatorname{val}(x)\rangle \mathbin{\Vert} \langle d\rangle)) + \operatorname{eval}(\operatorname{Lean\mathord{:}Omega\mathord{:}Linear\,Combo\mathord{:}mk}(1,\allowbreak{} \langle\,\rangle),\allowbreak{} \operatorname{of\,List}(\langle \operatorname{val}(x)\rangle \mathbin{\Vert} \langle d\rangle))\).
Using \paperlink{premise:4c65616e2e4f6d6567612e4c696e656172436f6d626f2e6164645f6576616c}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4c65616e2e4f6d6567612e4c696e656172436f6d626f2e6164645f6576616c}} with \(l_{1} = \operatorname{Lean\mathord{:}Omega\mathord{:}Linear\,Combo\mathord{:}mk}(0,\allowbreak{} \langle 0\rangle \mathbin{\Vert} \langle 1\rangle)\) and \(l_{2} = \operatorname{Lean\mathord{:}Omega\mathord{:}Linear\,Combo\mathord{:}mk}(1,\allowbreak{} \langle\,\rangle)\) and \(v = \operatorname{of\,List}\left(\langle \operatorname{val}(x)\rangle \mathbin{\Vert} \langle d\rangle\right)\) in reverse gives \(\operatorname{eval}(\operatorname{Lean\mathord{:}Omega\mathord{:}Linear\,Combo\mathord{:}mk}(0,\allowbreak{} \langle 0\rangle \mathbin{\Vert} \langle 1\rangle),\allowbreak{} \operatorname{of\,List}(\langle \operatorname{val}(x)\rangle \mathbin{\Vert} \langle d\rangle)) + \operatorname{eval}(\operatorname{Lean\mathord{:}Omega\mathord{:}Linear\,Combo\mathord{:}mk}(1,\allowbreak{} \langle\,\rangle),\allowbreak{} \operatorname{of\,List}(\langle \operatorname{val}(x)\rangle \mathbin{\Vert} \langle d\rangle)) = \operatorname{eval}(\operatorname{Lean\mathord{:}Omega\mathord{:}Linear\,Combo\mathord{:}mk}(0,\allowbreak{} \langle 0\rangle \mathbin{\Vert} \langle 1\rangle) + \operatorname{Lean\mathord{:}Omega\mathord{:}Linear\,Combo\mathord{:}mk}(1,\allowbreak{} \langle\,\rangle),\allowbreak{} \operatorname{of\,List}(\langle \operatorname{val}(x)\rangle \mathbin{\Vert} \langle d\rangle))\).
Chaining the equalities, we obtain \(d + 1 = \operatorname{eval}(\operatorname{Lean\mathord{:}Omega\mathord{:}Linear\,Combo\mathord{:}mk}(0,\allowbreak{} \langle 0\rangle \mathbin{\Vert} \langle 1\rangle) + \operatorname{Lean\mathord{:}Omega\mathord{:}Linear\,Combo\mathord{:}mk}(1,\allowbreak{} \langle\,\rangle),\allowbreak{} \operatorname{of\,List}(\langle \operatorname{val}(x)\rangle \mathbin{\Vert} \langle d\rangle))\).
Chaining the equalities, we obtain \(d + 1 = \operatorname{eval}(\operatorname{Lean\mathord{:}Omega\mathord{:}Linear\,Combo\mathord{:}mk}(0,\allowbreak{} \langle 0\rangle \mathbin{\Vert} \langle 1\rangle) + \operatorname{Lean\mathord{:}Omega\mathord{:}Linear\,Combo\mathord{:}mk}(1,\allowbreak{} \langle\,\rangle),\allowbreak{} \operatorname{of\,List}(\langle \operatorname{val}(x)\rangle \mathbin{\Vert} \langle d\rangle))\).
Using \paperlink{premise:4c65616e2e4f6d6567612e4c696e656172436f6d626f2e636f6f7264696e6174655f6576616c5f30}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4c65616e2e4f6d6567612e4c696e656172436f6d626f2e636f6f7264696e6174655f6576616c5f30}} in reverse gives \(\operatorname{val}(x) = \operatorname{eval}(\operatorname{coordinate}(0),\allowbreak{} \operatorname{of\,List}(\langle \operatorname{val}(x)\rangle \mathbin{\Vert} \langle d\rangle))\).
By definition, we obtain \(1 = \operatorname{eval}(\operatorname{Lean\mathord{:}Omega\mathord{:}Linear\,Combo\mathord{:}mk}(1,\allowbreak{} \langle\,\rangle),\allowbreak{} \operatorname{of\,List}(\langle \operatorname{val}(x)\rangle \mathbin{\Vert} \langle d\rangle))\).
By \paperlink{premise:4c65616e2e4f6d6567612e496e742e6164645f636f6e6772}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4c65616e2e4f6d6567612e496e742e6164645f636f6e6772}}, we obtain \(\operatorname{val}(x) + 1 = \operatorname{eval}(\operatorname{coordinate}(0),\allowbreak{} \operatorname{of\,List}(\langle \operatorname{val}(x)\rangle \mathbin{\Vert} \langle d\rangle)) + \operatorname{eval}(\operatorname{Lean\mathord{:}Omega\mathord{:}Linear\,Combo\mathord{:}mk}(1,\allowbreak{} \langle\,\rangle),\allowbreak{} \operatorname{of\,List}(\langle \operatorname{val}(x)\rangle \mathbin{\Vert} \langle d\rangle))\).
Using \paperlink{premise:4c65616e2e4f6d6567612e4c696e656172436f6d626f2e6164645f6576616c}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4c65616e2e4f6d6567612e4c696e656172436f6d626f2e6164645f6576616c}} with \(l_{1} = \operatorname{Lean\mathord{:}Omega\mathord{:}Linear\,Combo\mathord{:}mk}(0,\allowbreak{} \langle 1\rangle)\) and \(l_{2} = \operatorname{Lean\mathord{:}Omega\mathord{:}Linear\,Combo\mathord{:}mk}(1,\allowbreak{} \langle\,\rangle)\) and \(v = \operatorname{of\,List}\left(\langle \operatorname{val}(x)\rangle \mathbin{\Vert} \langle d\rangle\right)\) in reverse gives \(\operatorname{eval}(\operatorname{Lean\mathord{:}Omega\mathord{:}Linear\,Combo\mathord{:}mk}(0,\allowbreak{} \langle 1\rangle),\allowbreak{} \operatorname{of\,List}(\langle \operatorname{val}(x)\rangle \mathbin{\Vert} \langle d\rangle)) + \operatorname{eval}(\operatorname{Lean\mathord{:}Omega\mathord{:}Linear\,Combo\mathord{:}mk}(1,\allowbreak{} \langle\,\rangle),\allowbreak{} \operatorname{of\,List}(\langle \operatorname{val}(x)\rangle \mathbin{\Vert} \langle d\rangle)) = \operatorname{eval}(\operatorname{Lean\mathord{:}Omega\mathord{:}Linear\,Combo\mathord{:}mk}(0,\allowbreak{} \langle 1\rangle) + \operatorname{Lean\mathord{:}Omega\mathord{:}Linear\,Combo\mathord{:}mk}(1,\allowbreak{} \langle\,\rangle),\allowbreak{} \operatorname{of\,List}(\langle \operatorname{val}(x)\rangle \mathbin{\Vert} \langle d\rangle))\).
Chaining the equalities, we obtain \(\operatorname{val}(x) + 1 = \operatorname{eval}(\operatorname{Lean\mathord{:}Omega\mathord{:}Linear\,Combo\mathord{:}mk}(0,\allowbreak{} \langle 1\rangle) + \operatorname{Lean\mathord{:}Omega\mathord{:}Linear\,Combo\mathord{:}mk}(1,\allowbreak{} \langle\,\rangle),\allowbreak{} \operatorname{of\,List}(\langle \operatorname{val}(x)\rangle \mathbin{\Vert} \langle d\rangle))\).
By \paperlink{premise:4c65616e2e4f6d6567612e496e742e7375625f636f6e6772}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4c65616e2e4f6d6567612e496e742e7375625f636f6e6772}}, we obtain \(d + 1 - (\operatorname{val}(x) + 1) = \operatorname{eval}(\operatorname{Lean\mathord{:}Omega\mathord{:}Linear\,Combo\mathord{:}mk}(0,\allowbreak{} \langle 0\rangle \mathbin{\Vert} \langle 1\rangle) + \operatorname{Lean\mathord{:}Omega\mathord{:}Linear\,Combo\mathord{:}mk}(1,\allowbreak{} \langle\,\rangle),\allowbreak{} \operatorname{of\,List}(\langle \operatorname{val}(x)\rangle \mathbin{\Vert} \langle d\rangle)) - \operatorname{eval}(\operatorname{Lean\mathord{:}Omega\mathord{:}Linear\,Combo\mathord{:}mk}(0,\allowbreak{} \langle 1\rangle) + \operatorname{Lean\mathord{:}Omega\mathord{:}Linear\,Combo\mathord{:}mk}(1,\allowbreak{} \langle\,\rangle),\allowbreak{} \operatorname{of\,List}(\langle \operatorname{val}(x)\rangle \mathbin{\Vert} \langle d\rangle))\).
Using \paperlink{premise:4c65616e2e4f6d6567612e4c696e656172436f6d626f2e7375625f6576616c}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4c65616e2e4f6d6567612e4c696e656172436f6d626f2e7375625f6576616c}} with \(l_{1} = \operatorname{Lean\mathord{:}Omega\mathord{:}Linear\,Combo\mathord{:}mk}(1,\allowbreak{} \langle 0\rangle \mathbin{\Vert} \langle 1\rangle)\) and \(l_{2} = \operatorname{Lean\mathord{:}Omega\mathord{:}Linear\,Combo\mathord{:}mk}(1,\allowbreak{} \langle 1\rangle)\) and \(v = \operatorname{of\,List}\left(\langle \operatorname{val}(x)\rangle \mathbin{\Vert} \langle d\rangle\right)\) in reverse gives \(\operatorname{eval}(\operatorname{Lean\mathord{:}Omega\mathord{:}Linear\,Combo\mathord{:}mk}(1,\allowbreak{} \langle 0\rangle \mathbin{\Vert} \langle 1\rangle),\allowbreak{} \operatorname{of\,List}(\langle \operatorname{val}(x)\rangle \mathbin{\Vert} \langle d\rangle)) - \operatorname{eval}(\operatorname{Lean\mathord{:}Omega\mathord{:}Linear\,Combo\mathord{:}mk}(1,\allowbreak{} \langle 1\rangle),\allowbreak{} \operatorname{of\,List}(\langle \operatorname{val}(x)\rangle \mathbin{\Vert} \langle d\rangle)) = \operatorname{eval}(\operatorname{Lean\mathord{:}Omega\mathord{:}Linear\,Combo\mathord{:}mk}(1,\allowbreak{} \langle 0\rangle \mathbin{\Vert} \langle 1\rangle) - \operatorname{Lean\mathord{:}Omega\mathord{:}Linear\,Combo\mathord{:}mk}(1,\allowbreak{} \langle 1\rangle),\allowbreak{} \operatorname{of\,List}(\langle \operatorname{val}(x)\rangle \mathbin{\Vert} \langle d\rangle))\).
Chaining the equalities, we obtain \(d + 1 - (\operatorname{val}(x) + 1) = \operatorname{eval}(\operatorname{Lean\mathord{:}Omega\mathord{:}Linear\,Combo\mathord{:}mk}(1,\allowbreak{} \langle 0\rangle \mathbin{\Vert} \langle 1\rangle) - \operatorname{Lean\mathord{:}Omega\mathord{:}Linear\,Combo\mathord{:}mk}(1,\allowbreak{} \langle 1\rangle),\allowbreak{} \operatorname{of\,List}(\langle \operatorname{val}(x)\rangle \mathbin{\Vert} \langle d\rangle))\).

Use the previously proved fact \(\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}\left(\operatorname{val}(i)\right) + 1 \ne 0\).

Using \paperlink{premise:4e61742e636173745f616464}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4e61742e636173745f616464}} with \(R = \mathbb{Q}\) and \(m = d - \operatorname{val}(i)\) and \(1\) in place of its parameter \(n\) in reverse gives \(\operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(d - \operatorname{val}(i)) + \operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(1) = \operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(d - \operatorname{val}(i) + 1)\).
Chaining the equalities, we obtain \(\operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(d) - \operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(\operatorname{val}(i)) + \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(1) = \operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(d - \operatorname{val}(i) + 1)\).
Applying the same function to equal arguments, we obtain \((\operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(d) - \operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(\operatorname{val}(i)) + \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(1)) \cdot \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(\binom{2 \cdot (d - \operatorname{val}(i) + 1)}{d - \operatorname{val}(i) + 1}) = \operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(d - \operatorname{val}(i) + 1) \cdot \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(\binom{2 \cdot (d - \operatorname{val}(i) + 1)}{d - \operatorname{val}(i) + 1})\).
Using \paperlink{premise:4e61742e636173745f6d756c}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4e61742e636173745f6d756c}} with \(\alpha = \mathbb{Q}\) and \(m = d - \operatorname{val}(i) + 1\) and \(\binom{2 \cdot \left(d - \operatorname{val}(i) + 1\right)}{d - \operatorname{val}(i) + 1}\) in place of its parameter \(n\) in reverse gives \(\operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(d - \operatorname{val}(i) + 1) \cdot \operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(\binom{2 \cdot (d - \operatorname{val}(i) + 1)}{d - \operatorname{val}(i) + 1}) = \operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}((d - \operatorname{val}(i) + 1) \cdot \binom{2 \cdot (d - \operatorname{val}(i) + 1)}{d - \operatorname{val}(i) + 1})\).
Chaining the equalities, we obtain \((\operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(d) - \operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(\operatorname{val}(i)) + \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(1)) \cdot \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(\binom{2 \cdot (d - \operatorname{val}(i) + 1)}{d - \operatorname{val}(i) + 1}) = \operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}((d - \operatorname{val}(i) + 1) \cdot \binom{2 \cdot (d - \operatorname{val}(i) + 1)}{d - \operatorname{val}(i) + 1})\).
Applying the same function to equal arguments, we obtain \((y \mapsto (\operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(d) - \operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(\operatorname{val}(i)) + \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(1)) \cdot \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(\binom{2 \cdot (d - \operatorname{val}(i) + 1)}{d - \operatorname{val}(i) + 1}) = y) = (y \mapsto \operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}((d - \operatorname{val}(i) + 1) \cdot \binom{2 \cdot (d - \operatorname{val}(i) + 1)}{d - \operatorname{val}(i) + 1}) = y)\).
By \paperlink{premise:46696e2e69735f6c65}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:46696e2e69735f6c65}}, we obtain \(\operatorname{val}(i) \le d\).
By \paperlink{premise:4e61742e636173745f737562}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4e61742e636173745f737562}} with \(R = \mathbb{Q}\), we obtain \(\operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(d - \operatorname{val}(i)) = \operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(d) - \operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(\operatorname{val}(i))\).
Reversing the equality, we obtain \(\operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(d) - \operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(\operatorname{val}(i)) = \operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(d - \operatorname{val}(i))\).
Applying the same function to equal arguments, we obtain \(\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(2) \cdot (\operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(d) - \operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(\operatorname{val}(i))) = \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(2) \cdot \operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(d - \operatorname{val}(i))\).
Using \paperlink{premise:4e61742e636173745f6d756c}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4e61742e636173745f6d756c}} with \(\alpha = \mathbb{Q}\) and \(m = 2\) and \(d - \operatorname{val}(i)\) in place of its parameter \(n\) in reverse gives \(\operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(2) \cdot \operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(d - \operatorname{val}(i)) = \operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(2 \cdot (d - \operatorname{val}(i)))\).
Chaining the equalities, we obtain \(\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(2) \cdot (\operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(d) - \operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(\operatorname{val}(i))) = \operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(2 \cdot (d - \operatorname{val}(i)))\).
Applying the same function to equal arguments, we obtain \(\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(2) \cdot (\operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(d) - \operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(\operatorname{val}(i))) + \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(1) = \operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(2 \cdot (d - \operatorname{val}(i))) + \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(1)\).
Using \paperlink{premise:4e61742e636173745f616464}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4e61742e636173745f616464}} with \(R = \mathbb{Q}\) and \(m = 2 \cdot \left(d - \operatorname{val}(i)\right)\) and \(1\) in place of its parameter \(n\) in reverse gives \(\operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(2 \cdot (d - \operatorname{val}(i))) + \operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(1) = \operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(2 \cdot (d - \operatorname{val}(i)) + 1)\).
Chaining the equalities, we obtain \(\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(2) \cdot (\operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(d) - \operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(\operatorname{val}(i))) + \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(1) = \operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(2 \cdot (d - \operatorname{val}(i)) + 1)\).
Applying the same function to equal arguments, we obtain \(\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(2) \cdot (\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(2) \cdot (\operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(d) - \operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(\operatorname{val}(i))) + \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(1)) = \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(2) \cdot \operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(2 \cdot (d - \operatorname{val}(i)) + 1)\).
Using \paperlink{premise:4e61742e636173745f6d756c}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4e61742e636173745f6d756c}} with \(\alpha = \mathbb{Q}\) and \(m = 2\) and \(2 \cdot \left(d - \operatorname{val}(i)\right) + 1\) in place of its parameter \(n\) in reverse gives \(\operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(2) \cdot \operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(2 \cdot (d - \operatorname{val}(i)) + 1) = \operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(2 \cdot (2 \cdot (d - \operatorname{val}(i)) + 1))\).
Chaining the equalities, we obtain \(\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(2) \cdot (\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(2) \cdot (\operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(d) - \operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(\operatorname{val}(i))) + \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(1)) = \operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(2 \cdot (2 \cdot (d - \operatorname{val}(i)) + 1))\).
Applying the same function to equal arguments, we obtain \(\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(2) \cdot (\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(2) \cdot (\operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(d) - \operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(\operatorname{val}(i))) + \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(1)) \cdot \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(\binom{2 \cdot (d - \operatorname{val}(i))}{d - \operatorname{val}(i)}) = \operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(2 \cdot (2 \cdot (d - \operatorname{val}(i)) + 1)) \cdot \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(\binom{2 \cdot (d - \operatorname{val}(i))}{d - \operatorname{val}(i)})\).
Using \paperlink{premise:4e61742e636173745f6d756c}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4e61742e636173745f6d756c}} with \(\alpha = \mathbb{Q}\) and \(m = 2 \cdot \left(2 \cdot \left(d - \operatorname{val}(i)\right) + 1\right)\) and \(\binom{2 \cdot \left(d - \operatorname{val}(i)\right)}{d - \operatorname{val}(i)}\) in place of its parameter \(n\) in reverse gives \(\operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(2 \cdot (2 \cdot (d - \operatorname{val}(i)) + 1)) \cdot \operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(\binom{2 \cdot (d - \operatorname{val}(i))}{d - \operatorname{val}(i)}) = \operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(2 \cdot (2 \cdot (d - \operatorname{val}(i)) + 1) \cdot \binom{2 \cdot (d - \operatorname{val}(i))}{d - \operatorname{val}(i)})\).
Chaining the equalities, we obtain \(\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(2) \cdot (\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(2) \cdot (\operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(d) - \operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(\operatorname{val}(i))) + \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(1)) \cdot \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(\binom{2 \cdot (d - \operatorname{val}(i))}{d - \operatorname{val}(i)}) = \operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(2 \cdot (2 \cdot (d - \operatorname{val}(i)) + 1) \cdot \binom{2 \cdot (d - \operatorname{val}(i))}{d - \operatorname{val}(i)})\).
By congruence, we obtain \(((\operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(d) - \operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(\operatorname{val}(i)) + \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(1)) \cdot \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(\binom{2 \cdot (d - \operatorname{val}(i) + 1)}{d - \operatorname{val}(i) + 1}) = \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(2) \cdot (\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(2) \cdot (\operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(d) - \operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(\operatorname{val}(i))) + \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(1)) \cdot \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(\binom{2 \cdot (d - \operatorname{val}(i))}{d - \operatorname{val}(i)})) = (\operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}((d - \operatorname{val}(i) + 1) \cdot \binom{2 \cdot (d - \operatorname{val}(i) + 1)}{d - \operatorname{val}(i) + 1}) = \operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(2 \cdot (2 \cdot (d - \operatorname{val}(i)) + 1) \cdot \binom{2 \cdot (d - \operatorname{val}(i))}{d - \operatorname{val}(i)}))\).
By \paperlink{premise:5261742e696e7374436861725a65726f}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:5261742e696e7374436861725a65726f}}, we obtain \(\operatorname{Char\,Zero}\left(\mathbb{Q}\right)\).
By \paperlink{premise:4e61742e636173745f696e6a}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4e61742e636173745f696e6a}} with \(R = \mathbb{Q}\), we obtain \(\operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}((d - \operatorname{val}(i) + 1) \cdot \binom{2 \cdot (d - \operatorname{val}(i) + 1)}{d - \operatorname{val}(i) + 1}) = \operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(2 \cdot (2 \cdot (d - \operatorname{val}(i)) + 1) \cdot \binom{2 \cdot (d - \operatorname{val}(i))}{d - \operatorname{val}(i)}) \iff (d - \operatorname{val}(i) + 1) \cdot \binom{2 \cdot (d - \operatorname{val}(i) + 1)}{d - \operatorname{val}(i) + 1} = 2 \cdot (2 \cdot (d - \operatorname{val}(i)) + 1) \cdot \binom{2 \cdot (d - \operatorname{val}(i))}{d - \operatorname{val}(i)}\).
By propositional extensionality, we obtain \((\operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}((d - \operatorname{val}(i) + 1) \cdot \binom{2 \cdot (d - \operatorname{val}(i) + 1)}{d - \operatorname{val}(i) + 1}) = \operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(2 \cdot (2 \cdot (d - \operatorname{val}(i)) + 1) \cdot \binom{2 \cdot (d - \operatorname{val}(i))}{d - \operatorname{val}(i)})) = ((d - \operatorname{val}(i) + 1) \cdot \binom{2 \cdot (d - \operatorname{val}(i) + 1)}{d - \operatorname{val}(i) + 1} = 2 \cdot (2 \cdot (d - \operatorname{val}(i)) + 1) \cdot \binom{2 \cdot (d - \operatorname{val}(i))}{d - \operatorname{val}(i)})\).
Chaining the equalities, we obtain \(((\operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(d) - \operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(\operatorname{val}(i)) + \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(1)) \cdot \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(\binom{2 \cdot (d - \operatorname{val}(i) + 1)}{d - \operatorname{val}(i) + 1}) = \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(2) \cdot (\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(2) \cdot (\operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(d) - \operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(\operatorname{val}(i))) + \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(1)) \cdot \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(\binom{2 \cdot (d - \operatorname{val}(i))}{d - \operatorname{val}(i)})) = ((d - \operatorname{val}(i) + 1) \cdot \binom{2 \cdot (d - \operatorname{val}(i) + 1)}{d - \operatorname{val}(i) + 1} = 2 \cdot (2 \cdot (d - \operatorname{val}(i)) + 1) \cdot \binom{2 \cdot (d - \operatorname{val}(i))}{d - \operatorname{val}(i)})\).
Chaining the equalities, we obtain \(((\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(d) - \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(\operatorname{val}(i)) + 1) \cdot \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(\binom{2 \cdot (d - \operatorname{val}(i) + 1)}{d - \operatorname{val}(i) + 1}) = \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(2) \cdot (\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(2) \cdot (\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(d) - \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(\operatorname{val}(i))) + 1) \cdot \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(\binom{2 \cdot (d - \operatorname{val}(i))}{d - \operatorname{val}(i)})) = ((d - \operatorname{val}(i) + 1) \cdot \binom{2 \cdot (d - \operatorname{val}(i) + 1)}{d - \operatorname{val}(i) + 1} = 2 \cdot (2 \cdot (d - \operatorname{val}(i)) + 1) \cdot \binom{2 \cdot (d - \operatorname{val}(i))}{d - \operatorname{val}(i)})\).
Reversing the equality, we obtain \(((d - \operatorname{val}(i) + 1) \cdot \binom{2 \cdot (d - \operatorname{val}(i) + 1)}{d - \operatorname{val}(i) + 1} = 2 \cdot (2 \cdot (d - \operatorname{val}(i)) + 1) \cdot \binom{2 \cdot (d - \operatorname{val}(i))}{d - \operatorname{val}(i)}) = ((\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(d) - \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(\operatorname{val}(i)) + 1) \cdot \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(\binom{2 \cdot (d - \operatorname{val}(i) + 1)}{d - \operatorname{val}(i) + 1}) = \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(2) \cdot (\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(2) \cdot (\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(d) - \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(\operatorname{val}(i))) + 1) \cdot \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(\binom{2 \cdot (d - \operatorname{val}(i))}{d - \operatorname{val}(i)}))\).
By \paperlink{premise:4e61742e737563635f6d756c5f63656e7472616c42696e6f6d5f73756363}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4e61742e737563635f6d756c5f63656e7472616c42696e6f6d5f73756363}} with \(d - \operatorname{val}(i)\) in place of its parameter \(n\), we obtain \((d - \operatorname{val}(i) + 1) \cdot \binom{2 \cdot (d - \operatorname{val}(i) + 1)}{d - \operatorname{val}(i) + 1} = 2 \cdot (2 \cdot (d - \operatorname{val}(i)) + 1) \cdot \binom{2 \cdot (d - \operatorname{val}(i))}{d - \operatorname{val}(i)}\).
Transporting the result along the equality, we obtain \((\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(d) - \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(\operatorname{val}(i)) + 1) \cdot \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(\binom{2 \cdot (d - \operatorname{val}(i) + 1)}{d - \operatorname{val}(i) + 1}) = 2 \cdot (2 \cdot (\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(d) - \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(\operatorname{val}(i))) + 1) \cdot \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(\binom{2 \cdot (d - \operatorname{val}(i))}{d - \operatorname{val}(i)})\).
The substitution uses the following argument.
\begin{proofstep}
By definition, we obtain \(1 + d - \operatorname{val}(i) = 1 + d - \operatorname{val}(i)\).
Rewriting with \paperlink{premise:6164645f636f6d6d}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:6164645f636f6d6d}} in the preceding result gives \(d + 1 - \operatorname{val}(i) = 1 + d - \operatorname{val}(i)\).
Rewriting with \paperlink{premise:4e61742e6164645f7375625f6173736f63}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4e61742e6164645f7375625f6173736f63}} and \paperlink{premise:46696e2e69735f6c65}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:46696e2e69735f6c65}} in the preceding result gives \(d + 1 - \operatorname{val}(i) = 1 + \left(d - \operatorname{val}(i)\right)\).
Rewriting with \paperlink{premise:4e61742e6164645f636f6d6d}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4e61742e6164645f636f6d6d}} in the preceding result gives \(d + 1 - \operatorname{val}(i) = d - \operatorname{val}(i) + 1\).
Use the previously proved fact \(d + 1 - \operatorname{val}(i) = d - \operatorname{val}(i) + 1\).
\end{proofstep}
Treat \(\operatorname{val}(i)\), \(\binom{2 \cdot \operatorname{val}(i)}{\operatorname{val}(i)}\), \(\binom{2 \cdot \left(d - \operatorname{val}(i)\right)}{d - \operatorname{val}(i)}\) and \(d\) as indeterminates. Expanding and collecting like terms gives the same 16 terms on both sides.

By \paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6d756c5f6f6e65}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6d756c5f6f6e65}} with \(R = \mathbb{Q}\) and \(a = \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}\left(2\right)\), we obtain \(\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}(2) \cdot \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}(1) = \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}(2)\).

By \paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6f665f6571}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6f665f6571}} with \(\alpha = \mathbb{Q}\), we obtain \((\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(\binom{2 \cdot (d + 1)}{d + 1}) \cdot (2 \cdot (\operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(d) + 1) - (\operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(d) + 1)) - \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(\binom{2 \cdot (d + 1)}{d + 1}) \cdot (-1 + -\operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(d))) \cdot (\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(d) + 2) = \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(\binom{2 \cdot (d + 1)}{d + 1}) \cdot (2 \cdot (\operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(d) + 1) \cdot (\operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(d) + 1 + 1))\).

(\paperlink{premise:6164645f706f73}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:6164645f706f73}}), \paperlink{premise:6469765f6d756c5f65715f6d756c5f646976}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:6469765f6d756c5f65715f6d756c5f646976}}, \paperlink{premise:4973556e69742e6d756c5f6469765f63616e63656c5f7269676874}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4973556e69742e6d756c5f6469765f63616e63656c5f7269676874}}, \paperlink{premise:6973556e69745f6966665f6e655f7a65726f}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:6973556e69745f6966665f6e655f7a65726f}}, \paperlink{premise:65715f66616c7365}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:65715f66616c7365}}, \paperlink{premise:6d756c5f6e655f7a65726f}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:6d756c5f6e655f7a65726f}} (using \(\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}\left(d\right) + 1 + 1 \ne 0\)), \paperlink{premise:49735374726963744f72646572656452696e672e6e6f5a65726f44697669736f7273}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:49735374726963744f72646572656452696e672e6e6f5a65726f44697669736f7273}}, \paperlink{premise:41646447726f75702e6578697374734164644f664c45}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:41646447726f75702e6578697374734164644f664c45}}, \paperlink{premise:6e6f745f66616c73655f65715f74727565}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:6e6f745f66616c73655f65715f74727565}}, \paperlink{premise:65715f6469765f696666}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:65715f6469765f696666}} (using \(\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}\left(d\right) + 2 \ne 0\)) and \paperlink{premise:6469765f65715f696666}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:6469765f65715f696666}} in the preceding result gives \(\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(\binom{2 \cdot (d + 1)}{d + 1}) \cdot \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(1) \cdot (2 \cdot \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(d + 1) - \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(d + 1)) / (2 \cdot \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(d + 1) \cdot (\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(d + 1) + 1)) - \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(1) \cdot \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(\binom{2 \cdot (d + 1)}{d + 1}) \cdot (2 \cdot \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(0) - \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(d + 1)) / (2 \cdot \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(d + 1) \cdot (\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(d + 1) + 1)) = \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(\binom{2 \cdot (d + 1)}{d + 1}) / (\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(d) + 2)\).
Rewriting with \paperlink{premise:4e61742e7375625f73656c66}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4e61742e7375625f73656c66}} and \paperlink{premise:4e61742e63656e7472616c42696e6f6d5f7a65726f}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4e61742e63656e7472616c42696e6f6d5f7a65726f}} in the preceding result gives \(\operatorname{gosper\,Catalan}(d + 1,\allowbreak{} d + 1) - \operatorname{gosper\,Catalan}(d + 1,\allowbreak{} 0) = \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(\binom{2 \cdot (d + 1)}{d + 1}) / (\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(d) + 2)\).
\end{proofstep}
By definition, we obtain \(\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(\binom{2 \cdot (d + 1)}{d + 1}) / \operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(d + 2) = \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(\binom{2 \cdot (d + 1)}{d + 1}) / \operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(d + 1 + 1)\).
Rewriting with \paperlink{premise:4e61742e636173745f6f6e65}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4e61742e636173745f6f6e65}} and \paperlink{premise:4e61742e636173745f616464}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4e61742e636173745f616464}} in the preceding result gives \(\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(\binom{2 \cdot (d + 1)}{d + 1}) / (\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(d) + 2) = \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(\binom{2 \cdot (d + 1)}{d + 1}) / (\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(d + 1) + 1)\).
Rewriting with the identity established above in the preceding result gives \(\operatorname{gosper\,Catalan}(d + 1,\allowbreak{} d + 1) - \operatorname{gosper\,Catalan}(d + 1,\allowbreak{} 0) = \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(\binom{2 \cdot (d + 1)}{d + 1}) / (\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(d + 1) + 1)\).
Rewriting with \paperlink{premise:4e61742e737563635f65715f6164645f6f6e65}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4e61742e737563635f65715f6164645f6f6e65}} in the preceding result gives the same displayed equality.
Rewriting with \paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e73756d5f72616e67655f737562}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:46696e7365742e73756d5f72616e67655f737562}} in the preceding result gives \(\sum_{0\le i<d + 1}(\operatorname{gosper\,Catalan}(d + 1,\allowbreak{} i + 1) - \operatorname{gosper\,Catalan}(d + 1,\allowbreak{} i)) = \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(\binom{2 \cdot (d + 1)}{d + 1}) / (\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(d + 1) + 1)\).
Rewriting with \paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e73756d5f72616e6765}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:46696e7365742e73756d5f72616e6765}} in the preceding result gives \(\sum_{i\in \operatorname{Fin}(d + 1)}(\operatorname{gosper\,Catalan}(d + 1,\allowbreak{} \operatorname{val}(i) + 1) - \operatorname{gosper\,Catalan}(d + 1,\allowbreak{} \operatorname{val}(i))) = \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(\binom{2 \cdot (d + 1)}{d + 1}) / (\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(d + 1) + 1)\).
By \paperlink{premise:5472616e732e73696d706c65}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:5472616e732e73696d706c65}} with \(\alpha = \mathbb{Q}\), we obtain \(\sum_{i\in \operatorname{Fin}(d + 1)}(\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(\binom{2 \cdot \operatorname{val}(i)}{\operatorname{val}(i)}) / (\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(\operatorname{val}(i)) + 1) \cdot (\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(\binom{2 \cdot (d - \operatorname{val}(i))}{d - \operatorname{val}(i)}) / (\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(d) - \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(\operatorname{val}(i)) + 1))) = \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(\binom{2 \cdot (d + 1)}{d + 1}) / (\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(d + 1) + 1)\).
By \paperlink{premise:5472616e732e73696d706c65}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:5472616e732e73696d706c65}} with \(\alpha = \mathbb{Q}\), we obtain \(\sum_{x\in \operatorname{Fin}(d + 1)}(\operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(\operatorname{catalan}(\operatorname{val}(x))) \cdot \operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(\operatorname{catalan}(d - \operatorname{val}(x)))) = \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(\binom{2 \cdot (d + 1)}{d + 1}) / (\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(d + 1) + 1)\).
Rewriting with \paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e73756d5f636f6e6772}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:46696e7365742e73756d5f636f6e6772}} and the identity established above in the preceding result gives \(\sum_{x\in \operatorname{Fin}(d + 1)}(\operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(\operatorname{catalan}(\operatorname{val}(x)) \cdot \operatorname{catalan}(d - \operatorname{val}(x)))) = \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(\binom{2 \cdot (d + 1)}{d + 1}) / (\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(d + 1) + 1)\).
Rewriting with \paperlink{premise:4e61742e636173745f73756d}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4e61742e636173745f73756d}} in the preceding result gives \(\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(\sum_{i\in \operatorname{Fin}(d + 1)}(\operatorname{catalan}(\operatorname{val}(i)) \cdot \operatorname{catalan}(d - \operatorname{val}(i)))) = \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(\binom{2 \cdot (d + 1)}{d + 1}) / (\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(d + 1) + 1)\).
Rewriting with \paperlink{theorem:2}{Lemma~\ref{theorem:2}} in the preceding result gives \(\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(\operatorname{catalan}(d + 1)) = \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(\binom{2 \cdot (d + 1)}{d + 1}) / (\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(d + 1) + 1)\).
\end{proofstep}
By strong induction, \(\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(\operatorname{catalan}(n)) = \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(\binom{2 \cdot n}{n}) / (\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(n) + 1)\).
By \paperlink{premise:4e61742e737563635f6476645f63656e7472616c42696e6f6d}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4e61742e737563635f6476645f63656e7472616c42696e6f6d}}, we obtain \(n + 1 \mid \binom{2 \cdot n}{n}\).
Using \paperlink{premise:4e61742e636173745f6f6e65}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4e61742e636173745f6f6e65}} with \(R = \mathbb{Q}\) in reverse gives \(1 = \operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}\left(1\right)\).
Applying the same function to equal arguments, we obtain \((\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(\operatorname{catalan}(n)) = \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(\binom{2 \cdot n}{n}) / (\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(n) + 1)) = (\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(\operatorname{catalan}(n)) = \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(\binom{2 \cdot n}{n}) / (\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(n) + \operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(1)))\).
Using \paperlink{premise:4e61742e636173745f616464}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4e61742e636173745f616464}} with \(R = \mathbb{Q}\) and \(m = n\) and \(1\) in place of its parameter \(n\) in reverse gives \(\operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(n) + \operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(1) = \operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(n + 1)\).
Applying the same function to equal arguments, we obtain \(\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(\binom{2 \cdot n}{n}) / (\operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(n) + \operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(1)) = \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(\binom{2 \cdot n}{n}) / \operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(n + 1)\).
By \paperlink{premise:5261742e696e7374436861725a65726f}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:5261742e696e7374436861725a65726f}}, we obtain \(\operatorname{Char\,Zero}\left(\mathbb{Q}\right)\).
Use the previously proved fact \(n + 1 \mid \binom{2 \cdot n}{n}\).
By \paperlink{premise:496e742e636173745f6469765f6f664e61745f636861725a65726f}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:496e742e636173745f6469765f6f664e61745f636861725a65726f}} with \(k = \mathbb{Q}\), we obtain \(\operatorname{Int\mathord{:}cast}(\binom{2 \cdot n}{n} / (n + 1)) = \operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(\binom{2 \cdot n}{n}) / \operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(n + 1)\).
Reversing the equality, we obtain \(\operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(\binom{2 \cdot n}{n}) / \operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(n + 1) = \operatorname{Int\mathord{:}cast}(\binom{2 \cdot n}{n} / (n + 1))\).
Chaining the equalities, we obtain \(\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(\binom{2 \cdot n}{n}) / (\operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(n) + \operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(1)) = \operatorname{Int\mathord{:}cast}(\binom{2 \cdot n}{n} / (n + 1))\).
Applying the same function to equal arguments, we obtain \((\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(\operatorname{catalan}(n)) = \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(\binom{2 \cdot n}{n}) / (\operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(n) + \operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(1))) = (\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(\operatorname{catalan}(n)) = \operatorname{Int\mathord{:}cast}(\binom{2 \cdot n}{n} / (n + 1)))\).
Using \paperlink{premise:496e742e636173745f6e617443617374}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:496e742e636173745f6e617443617374}} with \(R = \mathbb{Q}\) and \(\operatorname{catalan}\left(n\right)\) in place of its parameter \(n\) in reverse gives \(\operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(\operatorname{catalan}(n)) = \operatorname{Int\mathord{:}cast}(\operatorname{catalan}(n))\).
Applying the same function to equal arguments, we obtain \((\operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(\operatorname{catalan}(n)) = \operatorname{Int\mathord{:}cast}(\binom{2 \cdot n}{n} / (n + 1))) = (\operatorname{Int\mathord{:}cast}(\operatorname{catalan}(n)) = \operatorname{Int\mathord{:}cast}(\binom{2 \cdot n}{n} / (n + 1)))\).
Chaining the equalities, we obtain \((\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(\operatorname{catalan}(n)) = \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(\binom{2 \cdot n}{n}) / (\operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(n) + \operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(1))) = (\operatorname{Int\mathord{:}cast}(\operatorname{catalan}(n)) = \operatorname{Int\mathord{:}cast}(\binom{2 \cdot n}{n} / (n + 1)))\).
By \paperlink{premise:5261742e696e7374436861725a65726f}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:5261742e696e7374436861725a65726f}}, we obtain \(\operatorname{Char\,Zero}\left(\mathbb{Q}\right)\).
By \paperlink{premise:496e742e636173745f696e6a}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:496e742e636173745f696e6a}} with \(\alpha = \mathbb{Q}\), we obtain \(\operatorname{Int\mathord{:}cast}(\operatorname{catalan}(n)) = \operatorname{Int\mathord{:}cast}(\binom{2 \cdot n}{n} / (n + 1)) \iff \operatorname{catalan}(n) = \binom{2 \cdot n}{n} / (n + 1)\).
By propositional extensionality, we obtain \((\operatorname{Int\mathord{:}cast}(\operatorname{catalan}(n)) = \operatorname{Int\mathord{:}cast}(\binom{2 \cdot n}{n} / (n + 1))) = (\operatorname{catalan}(n) = \binom{2 \cdot n}{n} / (n + 1))\).
By \paperlink{premise:496e742e696e7374436861725a65726f}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:496e742e696e7374436861725a65726f}}, we obtain \(\operatorname{Char\,Zero}\left(\mathbb{Z}\right)\).
By \paperlink{premise:4e61742e636173745f696e6a}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4e61742e636173745f696e6a}} with \(R = \mathbb{Z}\), we obtain \(\operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Z}}(\operatorname{catalan}(n)) = \operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Z}}(\binom{2 \cdot n}{n} / (n + 1)) \iff \operatorname{catalan}(n) = \binom{2 \cdot n}{n} / (n + 1)\).
By propositional extensionality, we obtain \((\operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Z}}(\operatorname{catalan}(n)) = \operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Z}}(\binom{2 \cdot n}{n} / (n + 1))) = (\operatorname{catalan}(n) = \binom{2 \cdot n}{n} / (n + 1))\).
Chaining the equalities, we obtain \((\operatorname{Int\mathord{:}cast}(\operatorname{catalan}(n)) = \operatorname{Int\mathord{:}cast}(\binom{2 \cdot n}{n} / (n + 1))) = (\operatorname{catalan}(n) = \binom{2 \cdot n}{n} / (n + 1))\).
Chaining the equalities, we obtain \((\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(\operatorname{catalan}(n)) = \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(\binom{2 \cdot n}{n}) / (\operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(n) + \operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Q}}(1))) = (\operatorname{catalan}(n) = \binom{2 \cdot n}{n} / (n + 1))\).
Chaining the equalities, we obtain \((\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(\operatorname{catalan}(n)) = \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(\binom{2 \cdot n}{n}) / (\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(n) + 1)) = (\operatorname{catalan}(n) = \binom{2 \cdot n}{n} / (n + 1))\).
Use the previously proved fact \(\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(\operatorname{catalan}(n)) = \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(\binom{2 \cdot n}{n}) / (\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}cast}(n) + 1)\).
Transporting the result along the equality, we obtain \(\operatorname{catalan}\left(n\right) = \binom{2 \cdot n}{n} / \left(n + 1\right)\).
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}\label{theorem:6}
Let \(n\) be a natural number. Then \(\left(n + 1\right) \cdot \operatorname{catalan}\left(n\right) = \binom{2 \cdot n}{n}\).
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
By \paperlink{premise:4e61742e737563635f6476645f63656e7472616c42696e6f6d}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4e61742e737563635f6476645f63656e7472616c42696e6f6d}}, we obtain \(n + 1 \mid \binom{2 \cdot n}{n}\).
By \paperlink{theorem:5}{Lemma~\ref{theorem:5}}, we obtain \(\operatorname{catalan}\left(n\right) = \binom{2 \cdot n}{n} / \left(n + 1\right)\).
Reversing the equality, we obtain \(\binom{2 \cdot n}{n} / \left(n + 1\right) = \operatorname{catalan}\left(n\right)\).
By \paperlink{premise:4e61742e65715f6d756c5f6f665f6469765f65715f7269676874}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4e61742e65715f6d756c5f6f665f6469765f65715f7269676874}}, we obtain \(\binom{2 \cdot n}{n} = \left(n + 1\right) \cdot \operatorname{catalan}\left(n\right)\).
Reversing the equality, we obtain \(\left(n + 1\right) \cdot \operatorname{catalan}\left(n\right) = \binom{2 \cdot n}{n}\).
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}\label{theorem:7}
\(\operatorname{catalan}\left(2\right) = 2\).
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Rewriting with \paperlink{theorem:5}{Lemma~\ref{theorem:5}}, \paperlink{premise:6164645f7a65726f}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:6164645f7a65726f}}, evaluation of natural-number arithmetic and the quotient \(\left\lfloor\frac{6}{3}\right\rfloor = 2\) gives \(\operatorname{catalan}\left(2\right) = 2\).
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}\label{theorem:8}
\(\operatorname{catalan}\left(3\right) = 5\).
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Rewriting with \paperlink{theorem:5}{Lemma~\ref{theorem:5}}, \paperlink{premise:6164645f7a65726f}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:6164645f7a65726f}}, evaluation of natural-number arithmetic and the quotient \(\left\lfloor\frac{20}{4}\right\rfloor = 5\) gives \(\operatorname{catalan}\left(3\right) = 5\).
\end{proof}

\section{Tree}
\begin{lemma}\label{theorem:9}

\begin{center}
\(\displaystyle \operatorname{trees\,Of\,Num\,Nodes\,Eq}\left(0\right) = \{\operatorname{Tree\mathord{:}nil}\}.\)
\end{center}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Rewriting with \paperlink{defeq:4}{defining equation~D\ref{defeq:4}} gives \(\operatorname{trees\,Of\,Num\,Nodes\,Eq}\left(0\right) = \{\operatorname{Tree\mathord{:}nil}\}\).
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}\label{theorem:10}
Let \(n\) be a natural number. Then
\begin{center}
\(\displaystyle \operatorname{trees\,Of\,Num\,Nodes\,Eq}(n + 1) = \bigcup_{\mathit{ij}\in \operatorname{antidiagonal}(n)} \operatorname{pairwise\,Node}(\operatorname{trees\,Of\,Num\,Nodes\,Eq}(\pi_1(\mathit{ij})),\allowbreak{} \operatorname{trees\,Of\,Num\,Nodes\,Eq}(\pi_2(\mathit{ij}))).\)
\end{center}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
For the required implication, argue as follows.
\begin{proofstep}
Fix \(a\) in \(\operatorname{Tree}\left(\operatorname{Unit}\right)\).
The substitution uses the following argument.
\begin{proofstep}
Fix \(a_{1}\) in \(\operatorname{antidiagonal}\left(n\right)\).
By \paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e6d656d5f617474616368}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:46696e7365742e6d656d5f617474616368}} with \(\alpha = \mathbb{N} \times \mathbb{N}\) and \(s = \operatorname{antidiagonal}\left(n\right)\) and \(x = a_{1}\), we obtain \(a_{1} \in \operatorname{attach}\left(\operatorname{antidiagonal}\left(n\right)\right)\).
By \paperlink{premise:65715f74727565}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:65715f74727565}}, we obtain \((a_{1} \in \operatorname{attach}(\operatorname{antidiagonal}(n))) = \mathrm{True}\).
Applying the same function to equal arguments, we obtain \((y \mapsto a_{1} \in \operatorname{attach}(\operatorname{antidiagonal}(n)) \land y) = (y \mapsto \mathrm{True} \land y)\).
By \paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e6d656d5f6d6170}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:46696e7365742e6d656d5f6d6170}} with \(\alpha = \operatorname{Tree}(\operatorname{Unit}) \times \operatorname{Tree}(\operatorname{Unit})\) and \(\beta = \operatorname{Tree}\left(\operatorname{Unit}\right)\), we obtain \(a \in \operatorname{map}(\operatorname{Function\mathord{:}Embedding\mathord{:}mk}(x \mapsto \operatorname{node}(\operatorname{unit},\allowbreak{} \pi_1(x),\allowbreak{} \pi_2(x))),\allowbreak{} \operatorname{sprod}(\operatorname{trees\,Of\,Num\,Nodes\,Eq}(\pi_1(\operatorname{val}(a_{1}))),\allowbreak{} \operatorname{trees\,Of\,Num\,Nodes\,Eq}(\pi_2(\operatorname{val}(a_{1}))))) \iff (\exists a_{2}\, :\, \operatorname{Tree}(\operatorname{Unit}) \times \operatorname{Tree}(\operatorname{Unit}),\; a_{2} \in \operatorname{sprod}(\operatorname{trees\,Of\,Num\,Nodes\,Eq}(\pi_1(\operatorname{val}(a_{1}))),\allowbreak{} \operatorname{trees\,Of\,Num\,Nodes\,Eq}(\pi_2(\operatorname{val}(a_{1})))) \land \operatorname{coe}(\operatorname{Function\mathord{:}Embedding\mathord{:}mk}(x \mapsto \operatorname{node}(\operatorname{unit},\allowbreak{} \pi_1(x),\allowbreak{} \pi_2(x))),\allowbreak{} a_{2}) = a)\).
By propositional extensionality, we obtain \((a \in \operatorname{map}(\operatorname{Function\mathord{:}Embedding\mathord{:}mk}(x \mapsto \operatorname{node}(\operatorname{unit},\allowbreak{} \pi_1(x),\allowbreak{} \pi_2(x))),\allowbreak{} \operatorname{sprod}(\operatorname{trees\,Of\,Num\,Nodes\,Eq}(\pi_1(\operatorname{val}(a_{1}))),\allowbreak{} \operatorname{trees\,Of\,Num\,Nodes\,Eq}(\pi_2(\operatorname{val}(a_{1})))))) = (\exists a_{2}\, :\, \operatorname{Tree}(\operatorname{Unit}) \times \operatorname{Tree}(\operatorname{Unit}),\; a_{2} \in \operatorname{sprod}(\operatorname{trees\,Of\,Num\,Nodes\,Eq}(\pi_1(\operatorname{val}(a_{1}))),\allowbreak{} \operatorname{trees\,Of\,Num\,Nodes\,Eq}(\pi_2(\operatorname{val}(a_{1})))) \land \operatorname{coe}(\operatorname{Function\mathord{:}Embedding\mathord{:}mk}(x \mapsto \operatorname{node}(\operatorname{unit},\allowbreak{} \pi_1(x),\allowbreak{} \pi_2(x))),\allowbreak{} a_{2}) = a)\).
For the required implication, argue as follows.
\begin{proofstep}
Fix \(a_{2}\) in \(\operatorname{Tree}\left(\operatorname{Unit}\right) \times \operatorname{Tree}\left(\operatorname{Unit}\right)\).
By \paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e6d656d5f70726f64756374}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:46696e7365742e6d656d5f70726f64756374}} with \(\alpha = \operatorname{Tree}\left(\operatorname{Unit}\right)\) and \(\beta = \operatorname{Tree}\left(\operatorname{Unit}\right)\), we obtain \(a_{2} \in \operatorname{sprod}(\operatorname{trees\,Of\,Num\,Nodes\,Eq}(\pi_1(\operatorname{val}(a_{1}))),\allowbreak{} \operatorname{trees\,Of\,Num\,Nodes\,Eq}(\pi_2(\operatorname{val}(a_{1})))) \iff \pi_1(a_{2}) \in \operatorname{trees\,Of\,Num\,Nodes\,Eq}(\pi_1(\operatorname{val}(a_{1}))) \land \pi_2(a_{2}) \in \operatorname{trees\,Of\,Num\,Nodes\,Eq}(\pi_2(\operatorname{val}(a_{1})))\).
By propositional extensionality, we obtain \((a_{2} \in \operatorname{sprod}(\operatorname{trees\,Of\,Num\,Nodes\,Eq}(\pi_1(\operatorname{val}(a_{1}))),\allowbreak{} \operatorname{trees\,Of\,Num\,Nodes\,Eq}(\pi_2(\operatorname{val}(a_{1}))))) = (\pi_1(a_{2}) \in \operatorname{trees\,Of\,Num\,Nodes\,Eq}(\pi_1(\operatorname{val}(a_{1}))) \land \pi_2(a_{2}) \in \operatorname{trees\,Of\,Num\,Nodes\,Eq}(\pi_2(\operatorname{val}(a_{1}))))\).
Applying the same function to equal arguments, we obtain \((a_{2} \in \operatorname{sprod}(\operatorname{trees\,Of\,Num\,Nodes\,Eq}(\pi_1(\operatorname{val}(a_{1}))),\allowbreak{} \operatorname{trees\,Of\,Num\,Nodes\,Eq}(\pi_2(\operatorname{val}(a_{1})))) \land \operatorname{node}(\operatorname{unit},\allowbreak{} \pi_1(a_{2}),\allowbreak{} \pi_2(a_{2})) = a) = ((\pi_1(a_{2}) \in \operatorname{trees\,Of\,Num\,Nodes\,Eq}(\pi_1(\operatorname{val}(a_{1}))) \land \pi_2(a_{2}) \in \operatorname{trees\,Of\,Num\,Nodes\,Eq}(\pi_2(\operatorname{val}(a_{1})))) \land \operatorname{node}(\operatorname{unit},\allowbreak{} \pi_1(a_{2}),\allowbreak{} \pi_2(a_{2})) = a)\).
\end{proofstep}
By function extensionality, we obtain \((x \mapsto x \in \operatorname{sprod}(\operatorname{trees\,Of\,Num\,Nodes\,Eq}(\pi_1(\operatorname{val}(a_{1}))),\allowbreak{} \operatorname{trees\,Of\,Num\,Nodes\,Eq}(\pi_2(\operatorname{val}(a_{1})))) \land \operatorname{node}(\operatorname{unit},\allowbreak{} \pi_1(x),\allowbreak{} \pi_2(x)) = a) = (x \mapsto (\pi_1(x) \in \operatorname{trees\,Of\,Num\,Nodes\,Eq}(\pi_1(\operatorname{val}(a_{1}))) \land \pi_2(x) \in \operatorname{trees\,Of\,Num\,Nodes\,Eq}(\pi_2(\operatorname{val}(a_{1})))) \land \operatorname{node}(\operatorname{unit},\allowbreak{} \pi_1(x),\allowbreak{} \pi_2(x)) = a)\).
Applying the same function to equal arguments, we obtain \((\exists x\, :\, \operatorname{Tree}(\operatorname{Unit}) \times \operatorname{Tree}(\operatorname{Unit}),\; x \in \operatorname{sprod}(\operatorname{trees\,Of\,Num\,Nodes\,Eq}(\pi_1(\operatorname{val}(a_{1}))),\allowbreak{} \operatorname{trees\,Of\,Num\,Nodes\,Eq}(\pi_2(\operatorname{val}(a_{1})))) \land \operatorname{node}(\operatorname{unit},\allowbreak{} \pi_1(x),\allowbreak{} \pi_2(x)) = a) = (\exists x\, :\, \operatorname{Tree}(\operatorname{Unit}) \times \operatorname{Tree}(\operatorname{Unit}),\; (\pi_1(x) \in \operatorname{trees\,Of\,Num\,Nodes\,Eq}(\pi_1(\operatorname{val}(a_{1}))) \land \pi_2(x) \in \operatorname{trees\,Of\,Num\,Nodes\,Eq}(\pi_2(\operatorname{val}(a_{1})))) \land \operatorname{node}(\operatorname{unit},\allowbreak{} \pi_1(x),\allowbreak{} \pi_2(x)) = a)\).
By \paperlink{premise:50726f642e657869737473}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:50726f642e657869737473}} with \(\alpha = \operatorname{Tree}\left(\operatorname{Unit}\right)\) and \(\beta = \operatorname{Tree}\left(\operatorname{Unit}\right)\), we obtain \((\exists x\, :\, \operatorname{Tree}(\operatorname{Unit}) \times \operatorname{Tree}(\operatorname{Unit}),\; (x_{1} \mapsto (\pi_1(x_{1}) \in \operatorname{trees\,Of\,Num\,Nodes\,Eq}(\pi_1(\operatorname{val}(a_{1}))) \land \pi_2(x_{1}) \in \operatorname{trees\,Of\,Num\,Nodes\,Eq}(\pi_2(\operatorname{val}(a_{1})))) \land \operatorname{node}(\operatorname{unit},\allowbreak{} \pi_1(x_{1}),\allowbreak{} \pi_2(x_{1})) = a)(x)) \iff (\exists a_{2}\, :\, \operatorname{Tree}(\operatorname{Unit}),\; \exists b\, :\, \operatorname{Tree}(\operatorname{Unit}),\; (x \mapsto (\pi_1(x) \in \operatorname{trees\,Of\,Num\,Nodes\,Eq}(\pi_1(\operatorname{val}(a_{1}))) \land \pi_2(x) \in \operatorname{trees\,Of\,Num\,Nodes\,Eq}(\pi_2(\operatorname{val}(a_{1})))) \land \operatorname{node}(\operatorname{unit},\allowbreak{} \pi_1(x),\allowbreak{} \pi_2(x)) = a)((a_{2},\allowbreak{} b)))\).
By propositional extensionality, we obtain \((\exists x\, :\, \operatorname{Tree}(\operatorname{Unit}) \times \operatorname{Tree}(\operatorname{Unit}),\; (\pi_1(x) \in \operatorname{trees\,Of\,Num\,Nodes\,Eq}(\pi_1(\operatorname{val}(a_{1}))) \land \pi_2(x) \in \operatorname{trees\,Of\,Num\,Nodes\,Eq}(\pi_2(\operatorname{val}(a_{1})))) \land \operatorname{node}(\operatorname{unit},\allowbreak{} \pi_1(x),\allowbreak{} \pi_2(x)) = a) = (\exists a_{2}\, :\, \operatorname{Tree}(\operatorname{Unit}),\; \exists b\, :\, \operatorname{Tree}(\operatorname{Unit}),\; (\pi_1((a_{2},\allowbreak{} b)) \in \operatorname{trees\,Of\,Num\,Nodes\,Eq}(\pi_1(\operatorname{val}(a_{1}))) \land \pi_2((a_{2},\allowbreak{} b)) \in \operatorname{trees\,Of\,Num\,Nodes\,Eq}(\pi_2(\operatorname{val}(a_{1})))) \land \operatorname{node}(\operatorname{unit},\allowbreak{} \pi_1((a_{2},\allowbreak{} b)),\allowbreak{} \pi_2((a_{2},\allowbreak{} b))) = a)\).
Chaining the equalities, we obtain \((\exists x\, :\, \operatorname{Tree}(\operatorname{Unit}) \times \operatorname{Tree}(\operatorname{Unit}),\; x \in \operatorname{sprod}(\operatorname{trees\,Of\,Num\,Nodes\,Eq}(\pi_1(\operatorname{val}(a_{1}))),\allowbreak{} \operatorname{trees\,Of\,Num\,Nodes\,Eq}(\pi_2(\operatorname{val}(a_{1})))) \land \operatorname{node}(\operatorname{unit},\allowbreak{} \pi_1(x),\allowbreak{} \pi_2(x)) = a) = (\exists a_{2}\, :\, \operatorname{Tree}(\operatorname{Unit}),\; \exists b\, :\, \operatorname{Tree}(\operatorname{Unit}),\; (\pi_1((a_{2},\allowbreak{} b)) \in \operatorname{trees\,Of\,Num\,Nodes\,Eq}(\pi_1(\operatorname{val}(a_{1}))) \land \pi_2((a_{2},\allowbreak{} b)) \in \operatorname{trees\,Of\,Num\,Nodes\,Eq}(\pi_2(\operatorname{val}(a_{1})))) \land \operatorname{node}(\operatorname{unit},\allowbreak{} \pi_1((a_{2},\allowbreak{} b)),\allowbreak{} \pi_2((a_{2},\allowbreak{} b))) = a)\).
Chaining the equalities, we obtain \((a \in \operatorname{map}(\operatorname{Function\mathord{:}Embedding\mathord{:}mk}(x \mapsto \operatorname{node}(\operatorname{unit},\allowbreak{} \pi_1(x),\allowbreak{} \pi_2(x))),\allowbreak{} \operatorname{sprod}(\operatorname{trees\,Of\,Num\,Nodes\,Eq}(\pi_1(\operatorname{val}(a_{1}))),\allowbreak{} \operatorname{trees\,Of\,Num\,Nodes\,Eq}(\pi_2(\operatorname{val}(a_{1})))))) = (\exists a_{2}\, :\, \operatorname{Tree}(\operatorname{Unit}),\; \exists b\, :\, \operatorname{Tree}(\operatorname{Unit}),\; (\pi_1((a_{2},\allowbreak{} b)) \in \operatorname{trees\,Of\,Num\,Nodes\,Eq}(\pi_1(\operatorname{val}(a_{1}))) \land \pi_2((a_{2},\allowbreak{} b)) \in \operatorname{trees\,Of\,Num\,Nodes\,Eq}(\pi_2(\operatorname{val}(a_{1})))) \land \operatorname{node}(\operatorname{unit},\allowbreak{} \pi_1((a_{2},\allowbreak{} b)),\allowbreak{} \pi_2((a_{2},\allowbreak{} b))) = a)\).
By congruence, we obtain \((a_{1} \in \operatorname{attach}(\operatorname{antidiagonal}(n)) \land a \in \operatorname{map}(\operatorname{Function\mathord{:}Embedding\mathord{:}mk}(x \mapsto \operatorname{node}(\operatorname{unit},\allowbreak{} \pi_1(x),\allowbreak{} \pi_2(x))),\allowbreak{} \operatorname{sprod}(\operatorname{trees\,Of\,Num\,Nodes\,Eq}(\pi_1(\operatorname{val}(a_{1}))),\allowbreak{} \operatorname{trees\,Of\,Num\,Nodes\,Eq}(\pi_2(\operatorname{val}(a_{1})))))) = (\mathrm{True} \land (\exists a_{2}\, :\, \operatorname{Tree}(\operatorname{Unit}),\; \exists b\, :\, \operatorname{Tree}(\operatorname{Unit}),\; (\pi_1((a_{2},\allowbreak{} b)) \in \operatorname{trees\,Of\,Num\,Nodes\,Eq}(\pi_1(\operatorname{val}(a_{1}))) \land \pi_2((a_{2},\allowbreak{} b)) \in \operatorname{trees\,Of\,Num\,Nodes\,Eq}(\pi_2(\operatorname{val}(a_{1})))) \land \operatorname{node}(\operatorname{unit},\allowbreak{} \pi_1((a_{2},\allowbreak{} b)),\allowbreak{} \pi_2((a_{2},\allowbreak{} b))) = a))\).
By \paperlink{premise:747275655f616e64}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:747275655f616e64}} with \(p = \exists a_{2}\, :\, \operatorname{Tree}(\operatorname{Unit}),\; \exists b\, :\, \operatorname{Tree}(\operatorname{Unit}),\; (a_{2} \in \operatorname{trees\,Of\,Num\,Nodes\,Eq}(\pi_1(\operatorname{val}(a_{1}))) \land b \in \operatorname{trees\,Of\,Num\,Nodes\,Eq}(\pi_2(\operatorname{val}(a_{1})))) \land \operatorname{node}(\operatorname{unit},\allowbreak{} a_{2},\allowbreak{} b) = a\), we obtain \((\mathrm{True} \land (\exists a_{2}\, :\, \operatorname{Tree}(\operatorname{Unit}),\; \exists b\, :\, \operatorname{Tree}(\operatorname{Unit}),\; (a_{2} \in \operatorname{trees\,Of\,Num\,Nodes\,Eq}(\pi_1(\operatorname{val}(a_{1}))) \land b \in \operatorname{trees\,Of\,Num\,Nodes\,Eq}(\pi_2(\operatorname{val}(a_{1})))) \land \operatorname{node}(\operatorname{unit},\allowbreak{} a_{2},\allowbreak{} b) = a)) = (\exists a_{2}\, :\, \operatorname{Tree}(\operatorname{Unit}),\; \exists b\, :\, \operatorname{Tree}(\operatorname{Unit}),\; (a_{2} \in \operatorname{trees\,Of\,Num\,Nodes\,Eq}(\pi_1(\operatorname{val}(a_{1}))) \land b \in \operatorname{trees\,Of\,Num\,Nodes\,Eq}(\pi_2(\operatorname{val}(a_{1})))) \land \operatorname{node}(\operatorname{unit},\allowbreak{} a_{2},\allowbreak{} b) = a)\).
Chaining the equalities, we obtain \((a_{1} \in \operatorname{attach}(\operatorname{antidiagonal}(n)) \land a \in \operatorname{map}(\operatorname{Function\mathord{:}Embedding\mathord{:}mk}(x \mapsto \operatorname{node}(\operatorname{unit},\allowbreak{} \pi_1(x),\allowbreak{} \pi_2(x))),\allowbreak{} \operatorname{sprod}(\operatorname{trees\,Of\,Num\,Nodes\,Eq}(\pi_1(\operatorname{val}(a_{1}))),\allowbreak{} \operatorname{trees\,Of\,Num\,Nodes\,Eq}(\pi_2(\operatorname{val}(a_{1})))))) = (\exists a_{2}\, :\, \operatorname{Tree}(\operatorname{Unit}),\; \exists b\, :\, \operatorname{Tree}(\operatorname{Unit}),\; (a_{2} \in \operatorname{trees\,Of\,Num\,Nodes\,Eq}(\pi_1(\operatorname{val}(a_{1}))) \land b \in \operatorname{trees\,Of\,Num\,Nodes\,Eq}(\pi_2(\operatorname{val}(a_{1})))) \land \operatorname{node}(\operatorname{unit},\allowbreak{} a_{2},\allowbreak{} b) = a)\).
\end{proofstep}
The substitution uses the following argument.
\begin{proofstep}
Fix \(a_{1}\) in \(\mathbb{N} \times \mathbb{N}\).
For the required implication, argue as follows.
\begin{proofstep}
Assume \(a_{1} \in \operatorname{antidiagonal}\left(n\right)\).
By definition, we obtain \((\exists a_{2}\, :\, \operatorname{Tree}(\operatorname{Unit}),\; \exists b\, :\, \operatorname{Tree}(\operatorname{Unit}),\; (a_{2} \in \operatorname{trees\,Of\,Num\,Nodes\,Eq}(\pi_1(a_{1})) \land b \in \operatorname{trees\,Of\,Num\,Nodes\,Eq}(\pi_2(a_{1}))) \land \operatorname{node}(\operatorname{unit},\allowbreak{} a_{2},\allowbreak{} b) = a) = (\exists a_{2}\, :\, \operatorname{Tree}(\operatorname{Unit}),\; \exists b\, :\, \operatorname{Tree}(\operatorname{Unit}),\; (a_{2} \in \operatorname{trees\,Of\,Num\,Nodes\,Eq}(\pi_1(a_{1})) \land b \in \operatorname{trees\,Of\,Num\,Nodes\,Eq}(\pi_2(a_{1}))) \land \operatorname{node}(\operatorname{unit},\allowbreak{} a_{2},\allowbreak{} b) = a)\).
By \paperlink{premise:4966662e6f665f6571}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4966662e6f665f6571}}, we obtain \((\exists a_{2}\, :\, \operatorname{Tree}(\operatorname{Unit}),\; \exists b\, :\, \operatorname{Tree}(\operatorname{Unit}),\; (a_{2} \in \operatorname{trees\,Of\,Num\,Nodes\,Eq}(\pi_1(a_{1})) \land b \in \operatorname{trees\,Of\,Num\,Nodes\,Eq}(\pi_2(a_{1}))) \land \operatorname{node}(\operatorname{unit},\allowbreak{} a_{2},\allowbreak{} b) = a) \iff (\exists a_{2}\, :\, \operatorname{Tree}(\operatorname{Unit}),\; \exists b\, :\, \operatorname{Tree}(\operatorname{Unit}),\; (a_{2} \in \operatorname{trees\,Of\,Num\,Nodes\,Eq}(\pi_1(a_{1})) \land b \in \operatorname{trees\,Of\,Num\,Nodes\,Eq}(\pi_2(a_{1}))) \land \operatorname{node}(\operatorname{unit},\allowbreak{} a_{2},\allowbreak{} b) = a)\).
\end{proofstep}
By \paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e486173416e7469646961676f6e616c2e6d656d5f616e7469646961676f6e616c}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:46696e7365742e486173416e7469646961676f6e616c2e6d656d5f616e7469646961676f6e616c}} with \(A = \mathbb{N}\), we obtain \(a_{1} \in \operatorname{antidiagonal}\left(n\right) \iff \pi_1(a_{1}) + \pi_2(a_{1}) = n\).
By propositional extensionality, we obtain \((a_{1} \in \operatorname{antidiagonal}(n)) = (\pi_1(a_{1}) + \pi_2(a_{1}) = n)\).
